Professor of Philosophy and of English, Government University of Peking since 1917. Dean, 1922. Author. Outline of Chinese Philosophy, Vol. I, (1919) and of Shang Shi Hsi (1920), Essays, etc. Organized and edited a weekly review called "Na li," 1922. Chief promoter of the Chinese Renaissance Movement: — p. 1006 ([scan leaf 1057](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1057/mode/1up))

**Hu, SUH** see Hu Shih. — p. 1006 ([scan leaf 1057](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1057/mode/1up))

**Hu SZE-YI, (胡思義)** .—Kiangsi. Magistrate in Yunnan. Salt Commissioner for Chekiang, 1915. Supervisor of the Hwai An Customs (North Kiangsu), April 1917. — p. 1006 ([scan leaf 1057](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1057/mode/1up))

**Hu WEI-TEH, (胡維德)** ,—Chekiang. Minister to Russia, 1904. Junior Councillor, Board of Foreign Affairs, September, 1907. Minister to Japan, March, 1908. Star of 2nd Class, 1st Division, January, 1909, Junior Vice-President, Board of Foreign Affairs, May, 1910. Senior Vice-President, July, 1910. Adjoint Director-General of Revenue Council, July, 1910. Acting Minister of Finance, November, 1911. Appointed Acting Minister for Foreign Affairs in the Republican Government, pending the arrival from Europe of Lu Cheng-hsiang. Controller-General of the Revenue Council (Shuiwuchu), March, 1912. (He was nominated as Minister of Communications in July, but his name was rejected by the National Council.) Minister to France, Spain and Portugal, November 24, 1915. Minister to Japan, since 1920. Vice-Chairman, Committee on Foreign Intercourse, May 1923. — p. 1006 ([scan leaf 1057](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1057/mode/1up))

**Hu YEN-HUNG** see Hu Han-min. — p. 1006 ([scan leaf 1057](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1057/mode/1up))

**Hu Yuan-hui, (胡源滙)** —Chihli. Born 1881. Graduate of a Japanese University. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chief of the Printing Bureau of the Ministry of Finance, Dec. 1917. Chinputang. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**Hua Nan-kuei, (T. T. Hoa) (華南圭)** —Kiangsu. Prepared in Soochow. Studied in Peking National University and in France. Engineer, Peking-Hankow Railway, 1911. Member of Ministry of Communications. Managing-Director, Pien-Lo (Kaifeng-Loyang) Railway, 1919. Re-joined Peking-Hankow Railway, 1920. Has served on various committees in the Ministry of Communications. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**Huang Chang, (黃璋)** —Szechwan. Born 1878. A Graduate of a High School in Szechwan, and a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan and a member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. After returning from Japan he was selected as member of the Provisional Szechwan Assembly, and was a teacher of the High School and Law School in Szechwan. He established a Law School and a Finance School in Szechwan. Kunghotang Author of The Latest Method of Book-keeping. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**Huang Ch'iang, (黃強)** —Kwangtung. Born 1887. Obtained preparatory education in France. Studied in an aeronautics school in England. Graduated from the Peiyang military training school. Superintendent of Customs, Chaochow, Kwangtung and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Swatow since August 24, 1920. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**Huang Ch'ing-lan, (黃慶瀾)** —Shanghai. Born 1874. Studied in the Normal School Department of Nanyang College, Shanghai. Chief, Higher Procurator's Court, Hupeh, Chief, Shanghai Local Court, 1911 to 1912. Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Wenchow, Chekiang, 1918. Chin Hua Taoyin, December, 1918. Kuei-chi Taoyin since December, 1919. Author: Comments on the New Criminal Code. Fourth, Third, Second class Chiaho; Second class Tashou Chiaho; Third class Pao Kuang Chiaho and Fourth class Wenhu Decorations. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**Huang Chun, (黃濬)** —Fukien. Born 1891. Educated in the Imperial Language School, Peking. Chujen. Secretary, Ministry of Communications, 1913. Councillor, Ministry of Finance, 1914. Secretary since 1916. Secretary to the President's office, 1918. Was editor of a newspaper and member of Kunghotang and Chinputang. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**Huang Fu, (黃郛)** —Chekiang. Graduated from Army Cadet College, Japan. Served on Staff of General Chen Chi-mei, and the Kiangsu Tutuh. After a visit to Europe and America wrote two books on "Lessons of the European War and the Future of China," and "The World After the War." In 1921 was sent to study economic conditions in Europe and America, and joined the Chinese Delegation at Washington as High Adviser. On his return to China was appointed Director General of the Finance Commission. Member, College of Generals, Aug. 1922, Minister for Foreign Affairs February to April, 1923. Minister of Education, Sept. 1923, to Jan. 1924. First class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**Huang Hsi-ch'uan, (黃錫銓)** —Kwangtung. Born 1881. Studied in Japan. Was a Taotai in Kwangsi; Chinese Consul at New York, and then transferred to Peru. In 1912, was High Adviser to the Tutuh of Kwangtung, and Adviser to the Defence Commissioner there, and then became Speaker to the Kwangtung Assembly. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Author of Comment on the Commercial Treaty between Great Britain and China, Experiments on Agriculture and Forestry (six volumes) etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1912. — p. 1007 ([scan leaf 1058](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1058/mode/1up))

**HUANG HSIAO-CHIU, (黃霄九)** —Kwangtung. Born 1884. Studied three years in Japan. Secretary of Tutuh's office, Kwangtung, 1912. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Opened two newspaper offices, which were shut down by officials for promulgating revolutionary ideas, but was afterwards engaged as editor of Jen Chuan Pao. Kungmintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG JUNG-LIANG, (Huang Yungliang, or Y. L. Huang), (黃榮良)** —Anhwei. Born 1875. A.B. Studied in the University of Nanking, 1890-96. Teacher, University of Nanking, 1896-97, Dean, 1897-99. Studied Liberal Arts in Baker University, 1900-03, at Columbia University 1903-5. Second Translator, Chinese Legation, London, 1906-7. Consul in New Zealand, 1908-10. Consul-General in Australia (Melbourne) 1910-13. Assistant Secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1914-16. Special Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Chihli, November 4, 1916. Appointed Minister to Austria 1920. Third class Wenhu, Second class Tashou Chiaho and Third class Paokwang Chiaho decorations. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG K'AI-WEN, (黃開文)** —Kwangtung. Born 1865. A Graduate of Peiyang Telegraph College. Acting Intendant of Industry, Mukden, August, 1907. Degraded to Sub-Prefect, August, 1909. Supervisor of Customs, Hankow, December 1, 1912. Chief of the Hankow office of the Kin-Han Railway Company. Chief of Bureau of Rites in the President's Office. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG KUO-EN, (Wong Ko-un), (黃國恩)** —Kwangtung. Born 1887. Graduate, University of Wisconsin, U.S.A. LL.B. Law Professor, Futan University, Shanghai, 1911. Lawyer, one of the seven promoters who organized the first Bar Association of China, 1911. Secretary and Legal Adviser to Chou Tzu-chi, when the latter was Civil and Military Governor of Shantung, 1912-13. President of the Shantung Provincial College, 1912-13. Compiler, Ministry of Communications, 1913. Secretary, Ministry of Finance, 1914. Chief Auditor for Hupeh, Hunan, Anhwei and Kiangsi Salt Districts, with Headquarters at Hankow, 1914. Member of Customs Revenue Administration, Peking, 1915. Private Secretary to Tuan Chih-kuei, ex-Minister of War, 1917-20. Military Judge, Metropolitan Garrison Guards Headquarters, 1917-20. Lecturer of Peking Government University, 1917-21. Assistant Secretary to Ministry of Communications, 1921. Member of Salt Adminstration, 1921. Assistant Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, 1915-22. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG P'EI-SUNG, (黃培松)** —Fukien. Military Optimus, 1880. Brigade General, Ch'iung-Yen-chen, Kwangtung, July, 1906. Defence Commissioner, Fukien, July 24, 1913. Made Pei Wei Chiang Chun, Sept. 1922. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG SHIH-LUNG, (黃士龍)** —Defence Commissioner, Kwangtung, August 10, 1913. Rewards Commissioner of the Forces, Kwangtung, August 8, 1913. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG, Y. L** see Huang Jung Liang. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG YEN-P'EI, (黃炎培)** —Kiangsu. Born Sept. 6, 1879. Graduated from the Nanyang University. D. Litt. Vice-Chairman, Kiangsu Provincial Educational Association, and Chairman, China Vocational Education Association. Member of Kiangsu Provincial Assembly Author of various works on Commerce, Education and Travel. Appointed Minister of Education, Dec. 25, 1921, but did not accept office. Reappointed, June 12, 1922, in Dr. W. W. Yen's Cabinet. Second Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG YUAN-WEI, (黃元蔚)** —Kwangtung. Born 1884. Educated in Japan. For sometime, Secretary to the Civil Governor of Kirin, later successive- ly became member of Ministry of Finance. Bank of China, Bank of Communications. In the year 1920, member of Military Government in Canton as member of the Finance Department. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1008 ([scan leaf 1059](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1059/mode/1up))

**HUANG YUN-P'ENG** (黃雲鵬).—Szechwan. Born 1883. A Graduate of Tungwen College in Szechwan and of a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan, and of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. In the time of Revolution he organized the Minkuo Lien-ho Hui in Shanghai, and was editor of the Shanghai Ta Kung Ho Jih Pao. Was a member of the Szechwan Provisional Assembly, and afterwards Director of the Chunchang Bank of Chungking. Kunghotang. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**HUANG YUNGLIANG** see Huang Jung Liang. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**I TSUNG-KU'EI** (易宗夔).—Hunan. Born 1875. Studied in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan, representing Mongolia, and a Member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. One of the representatives sent by the provinces to request the opening of a Parliament. Was a member of the late Tzu Cheng Yuan, and was prominent in the impeachment of Prince Ching. Was connected with the Revolution started by Tang Chai-tsang. On the failure of Kang Yu-wei's reform scheme he retired into seclusion and wrote several volumes on the subject of Reforms, until the time when schools were started everywhere, when he took up teaching. While in Japan he edited a revolutionary periodical. Kuomintang. Author of Huang Hai Lou Essays and Poetry. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Re-elected Member of House of Representatives 1917. Chief of Law Compilation Bureau, 1923. Second Class Tashou Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**I WEN-KU'EI** (易文熾).—Kweichow. Born 1888. Educated in Japan. Chujen. Presiding judge, Yunnan Higher Court of Justice in 1917. Chief Procurator Yunnan since March 26, 1917. Procurator, Central Procurator's Court since Feb. 1920. Third class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**JAO HAN-HSIANG** (饒漢祥).—Hupeh. Studied in Japan, Secretary to Tutuh of Hupeh, Commissioner of Administration for Hupeh. Civil Governor of Hupeh. Secretary to President Li Yuan-hung. Appointed Chief of Bureau of Immigration, June 15, 1922. Third Order of of Merit. First Class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**JAO MENG-JEN** (Dr. Loe Meng Len), (饒孟任).—Kiangsi. Born 1882. Chinshih. Studied law in England. LL.D. Secretary to the President. President, Peking National Law College. Member of House of Representatives. Co-Director, Bureau of Currency. Chinputang. Second class Tashou Chiaho, Chiaho, decorations. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**JEN HUNG-CHUN** (H. C. Zen), (任鴻雋).—Szechwan. Born 1886. Studied in Japan, and Cornell University (U.S.A.), B.A. and Columbia University, A.M. Professor, Peking National University, 1920. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Education, 1921. Director of Szechwan Iron and Steel Works, 1922. President of the Science Society of China. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**JEN K'O-CHENG** (任可澄).—Kweichow. Chujen. Started a Normal School and a law school. Chief, Auditing Bureau for Kweichow. Governor of Yunnan, 1915. Civil Governor of Kweichow, 1920. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**JUAN CHUNG-CHIH** (阮忠植).—Anhwei. Born May 31, 1865. Has been in civil service in Kirin since 1896. Was Taoyin and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs of the Kirin-Changchun district. Taoyin and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs of the I-Lan district, Kirin since April 5, 1914. Appointed Head of Bureau of Gold Purchase in Kirin, June 1, 1920. Third class, Second class, Chiaho; Second class Tashou Chiaho and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 1009 ([scan leaf 1060](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1060/mode/1up))

**KAILACHING, Prince** see Kung Sang No Erh Pu. — p. 1010 ([scan leaf 1061](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1061/mode/1up))

**K'ANG P'EI-HANG** (康佩珩).—Shansi. Born 1876. Studied in Japan after having graduated from Shansi University. At the end of a year he returned with several friends to start a revolution, but failing in his attempt in Mongolia, he was placed under arrest by the General there. Subsequently returned to his native place to do educational work. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. He does not belong to any party. Author of The Principle of Evolution, etc. — p. 1010 ([scan leaf 1061](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1061/mode/1up))

**K'ANG YU-WEI** (康有為).—Kwangtung. A well-known reformer, who started his campaign in favour of reform in the South by means of leaflets and lectures. Was received in audience by the late Emperor Kwang Hsu on June 14, 1898, on the recommendation of Weng T'ung-ho, the Imperial Tutor. He at once obtained a strong influence over the Emperor, whose famous reform decrees of 1898 were inspired by K'ang. Was proscribed, and ordered to be decapitated when the Empress Dowager effected her coup d'état, but escaped, and subsequently resided abroad, principally in America. An advocate of the adoption of Confucianism as the State Religion. Returned to China under the Republic. Was prominent in Peking at the time of Chang Hsun's restoration of the Manchu dynasty, July, 1917, and figured in the appointments made at that time. Now resides at Shanghai. — p. 1010 ([scan leaf 1061](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1061/mode/1up))

**KAO CHIA-CHI** (高家驥).—Heilungkiang. Born 1882. Graduated from Law Department, National Law College, Peking. Member, first Republican Parliament. Member, Constitution-Drafting Committee. Vice-Chairman, Provincial Assembly, Heilungkiang. — p. 1010 ([scan leaf 1061](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1061/mode/1up))

**KAO CHUNG-HO** (高仲和).—Hupeh. Born 1875. A Graduate of a Law School in Japan. After the Revolution he was appointed Councillor of the Tutuh's office, Hupeh. He led troops and captured several cities, and after the Declaration of the Republic he disbanded more than 4000 troops. He was editor of newspapers in Hupeh. A member of Tsan Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Author of Huifumong. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Member of Ministry of Interior. [illegible]fluence Tupan for Administration of Kiaochao, Mar. 18, 1924. — p. 1010 ([scan leaf 1061](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1061/mode/1up))

**KAO LIN-WEI** (高凌霨).—Chihli. Chujen. Provincial Treasurer for Hupeh under Chang Chih-tung. Was also Commissioner of Education, same province. Resigned after the revolution, 1911. Member of 1st Republican Parliament. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce in the first Chin Yun-ping cabinet formed after the Chihli-Anfu struggle, 1920. Minister of Finance, Oct. 28, 1921. Minister of Agriculture and Commerce in Dr. C. H. Wang's cabinet of 1922. Minister of Communications, May to June, 1922. Minister of Interior in Dr. W. W. Yen's cabinet. Appointed Civil Governor of Chihli, June 1922 but refused post. President of Commission for Study of Yangtzekiang Conservancy. Appointed Acting Premier by President Tsao Kun on Oct. 12, 1923, after having acted in that capacity for some time on the departure of Chang Shao Tseng from Peking, Sept. 1923. Released by the appointment of Sun Pao Chi, Jan. 12, 1924. Appointed Tupan of Shui Wu Chu (Director General of Custom Service), Jan. 12, 1924. — p. 1010 ([scan leaf 1061](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1061/mode/1up))

**KAO SUNG-JU** (高松如).—Chihli. Director-General, Chihli Provincial Bank and Industrial Taotai of Hupeh under Manchu Government; Director, Hupeh Government Bank; Co-Director, Hupeh Government Mint. Superintendent of Mines in Hupeh. — p. 1010 ([scan leaf 1061](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1061/mode/1up))

**KAO TSENG-CHUEH, (高增爵)** —Shensi. Chuhsi Police Intendant, Szechwan. spring, 1908. Pacificator in Shensi, November, 1911. Acting Civil Governor of Shansi, October 19, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan, 1914. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KENG CH'UN-YEN, (耿春宴)** —Honan. Born 1883. Studied in Japan. A member of the Central Educational Society, and a member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Teacher and Director of various schools. Chinputang. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KENG YEN** —Councillor, Department of Legislation. Cabinet, August 31, 1912. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KING, P. C** see Chin Pang Chêng. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KING, THOMAS** see Chin Chih. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**Koo, V. K. Wellington** see Ku Wei Chun. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KU CHUNG-HSIU, (谷鐘秀)** —Chihli. Born 1874. Graduate of the Peking Government University and a returned student from Japan. Was a Teacher in the High Normal School in Chihli. Was a Secretary to the Governor of Chekiang, establishing eight judicial courts in the Province. After the outbreak at Wuchang, was elected to proceed to Wuchang and Nanking to assist in the organization of the new Government, and was appointed to draft the Provisional Constitution, etc. Was a member of the late National Council, and of the Chung Yi Yuan, and member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Kuomintang. Author of Outlines of Chinese History, Moral Philosophy, The Essentials of a Constitution, Financial Reforms, and a book on Poetry, Translator of several works from foreign languages into Chinese. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Minister of Commerce, 1916. First class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KU HUNG-MING, (辜鴻銘)** —Born 1856 at Penang. Nationality, Chinese. For 17 years secretary to H.E. the late Viceroy Chang Chi-tung in Wuchang. For five years managing director of Whangpoo Conservancy Board, in Shanghai. For eight months Dean of Nanyang College in Shanghai. Author of many books (now all out of print), such as "Translations of Discourses and Sayings of Confucius," "The Conduct of Life," "Higher Education," "Papers from a Viceroy's Yamen," "The Story of a Chinese Oxford Movement," "Vox Clamantis." Four of these books have been translated into German and Swedish. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KU I-TS'ENG, (顧儀曾)** —Peking. Born Oct. 9, 1888. Graduated in the Law Department of Government Law School, Peking. Secretary, Ministry of Interior since Nov. 2. 1919. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KU MING-TE** —Sinkiang. Born 1869. Studied in Sinkiang High School, and a graduate of the school established by Tzu Yi-chu. Vice-Speaker of Sinkiang Provincial Assembly. Chinputang. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KU P'IN-CHEN, (顧品珍)** —Yunnan. Born 1881. Graduated from a Military School in Japan. Upon his return to China, Ku was engaged to train officers in Yunnan. Brigadier General of the 1st. Brigade and General of the 1st. Army Division. Joined the Canton Military Government and is the Commanding Officer of the Yunnan Allied Army. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KU WEI-CHUN, (Dr. V. K. Wellington Koo), (顧維鈞)** —Kiangsu. Ph.D. in Law, Columbia University, New York. Member of Phi Beta Kappa Society. Secretary to Cabinet, May, 1912; retired July, 1912. Secretary to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, August, 1912; Councillor, 1914. Minister Plenipotentiary to Mexico, 1915. Was Minister at Washington, till 1920, when was appointed Minister to London. Was a member of the Peace Conference of Paris, 1918-19. China's representative on the Council of League of Nations. Chinese Delegate to the Washington Conference, Nov. 1921. Visited China, May 1922. Appointed Chairman of the Commission for the Study of National Finance, June 1922, and Minister of Foreign Affairs Sept. to Dec. 1922. Again appointed to the same post, Aug. 5, 1922 in Dr. Tang Shao-yi's cabinet. Reappointed Sept. 19th, (Dr. C. H. Wang's Cabinet). Was appointed President, Commission for Study of National Finance by President Li but refused to take office which was offered to Chang Yin Hua in Sept. 1922. Minister of Foreign Affairs in the Kao Lin-wei Cabinet April 9, 1923. Again, in Sun Pao-chi's cabinet, Jan. 12, 1924. Second order of Merit. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 1011 ([scan leaf 1062](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1062/mode/1up))

**KU YUAN** —Secretary, Ministry of the Interior, June, 1912. — p. 1012 ([scan leaf 1063](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1063/mode/1up))

**KUAN HAI-CH'ING, (關海清)** —Mukden. Born 1882. Educated in the Imperial Peking University. Chujen. Was successively Director of the Mukden Provincial College. Law School, Language School. Secretary to the Military and Civil Governors Appointed Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Fengtien February 1918. Author of "Lecture Notes on Logic." Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1012 ([scan leaf 1063](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1063/mode/1up))

**KUAN KENG-LIN, (關賡麟)** --Kwangtung. Born November, 1880. Graduated in Japanese College. Studied Politics and Law in the Imperial Peking University for two and half years. Chinshih. Member of Board of Army 1903. Accompanied the Five Special Commissioners to study Government Systems in Europe and America, 1904. Member of Board of Communications, 1905. Assistant Director, Peking-Hankow Railway, 1910 Director, the same railway, 1912, to 1915. Member of Ministry of Finance, December, 1916. Department Chief, Ministry of Communications, July, 1917. Vice-Chairman and General Secretary, National Railway Association. Member, Finance Committee of the President's Office, Councillor Ministry of Communications, Jan. 1919. Chief, Publication Department in the same Ministry, July, 1919. Director, Hankow-Kwangtung-Szechwan Railway since July, 1920. President of Chiaotung University, June to Aug. 1922. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1012 ([scan leaf 1063](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1063/mode/1up))

**KUAN MIEN-CHUN, (關冕鈞)** —Kwangsi. Born 1870. A Chinshih and Hanlin Compiler of the late Dynasty. Was Vice-Director of the Ching-Chang Railway in 33rd year of Kwang Hsu, and was Director of the Ching-Chang and Chang-Sui Railways since the 3rd year of Hsuan Tung. Received a 5th class Chia-ho Order in the 2nd year of the Republic of China. In the 31st year of Kwang Hsu was appointed second-class secretary to proceed to the United States, Russia, Germany, Austria and Italy with the Investigation Commissioners to study the political situations abroad, and at that time he travelled in Japan, Great Britain, France, Belgium and several other countries. Superintendent, Sha-hukou Customs, (Kuei-hua-cheng). Superintendent of Tsaipei Customs. Resigned. June 2, 1922. — p. 1012 ([scan leaf 1063](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1063/mode/1up))

**KUANG CHIN-P'AN, (Chin P'on Kwang). (鄺錦津)** —Kwangtung. Born 1892. Studied commercial economics in University of Colorado; and railway engineering in Columbia University. Secretary to Chinese Consulate General at San Francisco, 1914. Was railway and bridge engineer in America. 1915-16. Assistant Engineer, Chow-Hsiang Railway, 1917. Joined the Tientsin-Pukow Railway, 1918. — p. 1012 ([scan leaf 1063](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1063/mode/1up))

**KUANG FU-SHAO, (Fong F. Sec.), (鄺富灼)** —Kwangtung. Born 1869. Studied in the Pomona College, University of California and Columbia University. M.A. Editor of the Commercial Press, Ltd., since 1908. Author, Joint-author and compiler of various text books, reference books, etc. — p. 1012 ([scan leaf 1063](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1063/mode/1up))

**KUANG SUN-MOU, (鄺孫謀)** —Kwangtung. Born 1863. A Graduate of Boston University. Engineer-in-Chief of the King-Chang and Chang-Sui Railways, and Superintendent of Workshops, since the 6th month of the 3rd year of Hsuan Tung. Has successively been Engineer, Engineer-in-Chief, etc., of Tsin-Pu, Outside Shanhaikuan, Ping-Li, Hsinmintun, Tientsin-Pukow Railways from 1885-1922. Received a 3rd class Chia-ho Decoration from the President. — p. 1012 ([scan leaf 1063](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1063/mode/1up))

**KUNG CHENG** (龔政).—Kwangsi. Born 1886. A Gratuate of the Japanese Imperial University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan and the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUNG CHI-PING** (龔積柄).—Anhwei. Born 1872. Chief, Shantung Higher Court. Salt Commissioner of Shantung, July 1912, Acting Civil Governor of Shantung, Feb. 1914. Taoyin in Shantung. Appointed Commissioner of Finance, Shantung, August 26, 1921. Second class Chiaho and Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUNG HSIN-CHEN** (龔心湛).—Anhwei. Attached to Chinese Legations abroad, and was Taotai in Kwangtung and Yunnan. Chief of the Salt Bureau, June, 1914. Concurrently Director of the Inspectorate General, 1915. Vice-Minister, Minister of Finance and ex-Premier. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUNG SANG NO ERH PU** (貢桑諾爾布) (Prince Kalaching).—Chief of the Bureau of Mongolia and Tibet, September, 1912, till April, 1922. Prince of the Right Wing of the Kharach'in Tribe of the Chosot'u League of Inner Mongolia. Promoted from Second to First class Prince in October, 1912, for loyalty to the Republic. Appointed Director, Bureau of Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs, Feb. 9, 1923. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUNG YUAN-K'AI** (龔元凱).—Anhwei. Born 1870. Graduated from Chin Shih Kuan. Hanlin 1903. Wei Ch'uan Taoyin, April, 1919. Author of T'o K'an Poems. Non-partisan. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUO CH'UNG-CH'U** (郭崇渠).—Szechwan. Born August 22, 1882. Graduated from the Szechwan Higher Normal School. Studied for some time in the Peking Law College. Vice-Chairman, Szechwan Provincial Assembly since July, 1919. Member of Kuomintang. Fifth class Wenhu decoration. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUO CHUNG-HSI** (郭宗熙).—Hunan. Civil Governor of Kirin, since June 1916. Appointed Chinese President of the Chinese Eastern Railway, 1918. Has been Taoyin in Kirin. Delegate of Foreign Affairs for Kirin. Educational Commissioner of Kirin. Now retired. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUO HSI-JEN** (郭希仁).—Shensi. Born Feb. 6, 1882. Studied in the Hongtao School and the Provincial College of Shensi. Chujen. Commissioner of Education for the Province of Shensi since April, 1920. Author of various works on irrigation, history, travel and diplomacy. Formerly member of Kuomintang. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUO KAN-CHING** (郭幹卿).—Chihli. Born April 23, 1883. Studied in the Peiyang Military school. Held various offices in the Army after graduation before becoming the Director of Government Mint of Hupeh in 1919 and Chief of Note-Issue Department of the National Currency Bureau in 1921. Secretary to the President's Office, 1922. Commissioner of Finance of Hupeh, since Dec. 1922. Second class Tashou Chiaho and 3rd class Wenhu decorations. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUO, DR. P. W** see Kuo Ping-wen. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**KUO PING-WEN** (P. W. Kuo), (郭秉文).—Shanghai. Born 1880. Graduated from Lowrie Institute, Shanghai, of which he became a teacher. Served in Customs service in Shanghai, Kashing and Hangchow. He went to America in 1906, and attended University of Wooster and Columbia University and received the degree of Ph.D. from the latter. After his return to China, he was editor in the Commercial Press and Dean of Government High Normal College, Nanking. Appointed Presi dent of the same college, and also President of Southeastern University and of College of Commerce, Nanking and Shanghai. — p. 1013 ([scan leaf 1064](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1064/mode/1up))

**Kuo Tai-chi, (郭泰祺)** —Hupeh. Born 1890. Was a government student in America. Upon graduation in 1910, he returned to China to became the secretary to Li Yuan Hung, then vice-President. He was Pres. Li's secretary during, 1916-7 and concurrently Councillor to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Now one of Sun Yat-sen's Secretaries. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Kuo Tse-yun, (郭則澐)** —Fukien. Born 1882. Hanlin of the late Ching Dynasty. Studied in Waseda University, Japan. Customs Taotai of Wenchow and Chuchow, Chekiang. Police Taotai of Hangchow, 1911. Was twice Chief Secretary to the Cabinet during the establishment of the Republic and in Chien Nun-hsun's Cabinet. Again appointed Chief Secretary to the Cabinet of Chin Yun-peng, in 1918. Director Bureau of Economical study 1920. Chief of Bureau of Immigration, Dec. 18, 1921. Resigned June 15, 1922. Author of Memoirs of Japan, Notes on Legal Subjects, and a collection of poems. First class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho and First class Wenhu decorations. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**KWONG, CHIN PON** see Kuang Chin'p'an. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**KWONG, K. Y** see Kuang Sun-mou. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Lan Chien-shu, (藍建樞)** —Fukien. Educated in the Naval College in Fukien. Senior member of Naval General Staff, April 1912. Commander-in-chief of Chinese Navy from March 28, 1918. Fourth Order of Merit. Second class Wenhu, Tashou Chiaho and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Lang Kuo-chen, (K. T. Long), (郎國楨)** —Kiangsu. Born 1888. Graduated from Nanyang College, Shanghai, and University of Wisconsin, U.S.A. M.A. Engineer, Tientsin Telephone Administration, 1913. Acting Chief, 1916. Sub-Manager, China Electric Co., Ltd., 1918 to date. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Lao Chih-ch'ang, (S. W. Lao), (勞之常)** —Shantung. Was Chief of the Shantung Conservancy Bureau. Chairman, Shantung International Relief Committee. Commissioner to the China International Famine Relief Commission. Appointed Vice-Minister of Communications, June 2, 1922. Ordered to assume charge of the Ministry, Dec. 22, 1922. Relieved of post Jan. 30, 1923. Appointed to assist Dr. C. T. Wang on the Commission for the Settlement of the Shantung Question, Nov. 1922. First class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**LAO, S. W** see Lao Chih-ch'ang. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**LAU, W. W** see Liu Huan-wei. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**LEE, YEW C** see Li Yao-hsiang. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**LEW YUK-LIN** see Liu Yu Lin. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Li Chao-chen, (李兆珍)** —Fukien. Governor of Anhwei, 1915. Reappointed Civil Governor of Anhwei, Autumn 1921, but was rejected by the people and held his office only a few days. Created Marquis, 2nd Order, December, 1915. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Li Chao-fu, (李肇甫)** —Szechwan. Born November 4, 1885. Studied Law in Japan. President, Szechwan Provincial Assembly since July, 1919. Member Kuomintang. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Li Cheng-fen, (李正芬)** —Yunnan. Born October 7, 1856. Chairman, Yunnan Provincial Assembly since September, 1918. Fourth Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**Li Chi-ch'eng, (李濟臣)** —Appointed Tupan of Military Affairs for Shantung, May 30, 1923. Civil Governor of Honan, Dec. 12, 1923. — p. 1014 ([scan leaf 1065](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1065/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-FANG, (李經芳)** —Anhwei. An adopted son of the late Marquis Li Hung-chang. Minister to Japan. Minister to Great Britain, May, 1907. Acting Senior Vice-President of the Ministry of Communications, January, 1911. Director-General of the Chinese Imperial Post Office. Retired in January, 1912. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CH'ING-FANG, (李慶芳)** —Shansi. Born 1878. A Graduate of Shansi University and a University in Japan. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan and member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee and editor of Hsien Fa Hsin Wen. Was Chairman of Educational Society of Shansi. Chinputang. Author of The Construction of a Republic, etc. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-HSI, (李鍾羲)** —Anhwei. Yung-Ning Tao, Szechwan, May, 1887. Salt Tao-tai, Hunan, August, 1893. Jud. Comm., June, 1897. Fin. Comm., Fukien, September, 1898, of Yunnan, January, 1899. Governor, Kwangsi, April, 1901, of Yunnan, June, 1901. Removed from office, May, 1902. Acting Governor of Kweichow, January, 1903. Governor, Kwangsi, May, 1904; retired, ill-health, October, 1905. Governor-General, Yun-Kuei Provinces, February, 1909. Member of the Tsan-chengyuan, 1915. Director of the Land Survey Office, 1915. "Friend of Sungshan" (see Hsu Shih-ch'ang). Elected Premier by both. Houses of Parliament, May, 1917, but failed to take up appointment before the dissolution of Parliament, June, 1917. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-HSI, (李景曦)** —Fukien. Educated in the Naval College, Foochow. Departmental Head, Ministry of Navy since July, 1917. Second class Wenhu and Third class Chiaho decorations. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-LIEN, (李景濂)** —Chihli. Born 1868. Educated in Leng Chi College, Chihli, and a Chinshih of the late Dynasty. Was a Teacher in Chinese at various High Schools and Universities. A member of Chung Yi Yuan. Author of several Chinese poetical and other works which have not yet been printed. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-MAI, (李經邁)** —Anhwei. Son of Li Hung-chang. Minister to Austria-Hungary, September, 1905-7: Jud. Comm., Honan, March 1908; resigned, November, 1908. Acting Junior Vice-President, Minchengpu, March, 1911. Acting Vice-Minister of Yu-chuanpu, January, 1912. Relieved of post, January, 1912. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-MING, (李景銘)** —Fukien. Secretary, Ministry of Finance, September 1, 1912. Auditor, Audit Bureau, November 22, 1913. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CHIU-YING, (李求膺)** —Shensi. Born 1883. A Graduate of the High School in Shensi, and a University in Japan. Was editor of the Min Lik Pao, Shanghai, and was a member of the Advisory Council in Nanking and Provisional National Council in Peking. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 5, 1913. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI CHUN, (李準)** —Szechwan. Brigade General, Nan-Ao Chen, Kwangtung, August, 1905. Acting Brigade General, Pakhoi, July, 1907. In charge of piracy suppression in Canton Waters, 1907-8. Admiral, Kwangtung, July, 1909. Commissioner for Pacification, Kwangtung, August 14, 1913. Created (Brevet) General, August 14, 1913, and Chiangchun, Oct. 1922. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI HAN-CH'ENG, (李漢丞)** —Hunan. Born 1867. Graduate of a University in Japan. Hsiutsai of the late Dynasty. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Established a girls' school in Hengshan. Senior Clerk of the Ministry of Industry and Commerce, July, 1912. Resigned, March, 1913. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1015 ([scan leaf 1066](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1066/mode/1up))

**LI HAN-FANG, (李含芳)** —Shensi. Secretary to the Tutuh of Shensi during the revolution. Elected member of Chung Yi Yuan. Principal of a school in his native place. Unseated by Presidential Order of Nov. 4, 1913. Returned to Peking to function as a member of the House, 1922. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI HAN-FANG, (李含芳)** —Shantung. Born Nov. 30, 1882. Graduated from the Science Department of the Higher Normal School, Peking. Vice-Chairman, Shantung Provincial Educational Association since July, 1917. Director, Girls' Normal School of Shantung since March, 1920, Fifth Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI HOU-CHI, (李厚基)** —Kiangsu. Born 1872. Commandant of Strategic Positions at Woosung, August 19, 1913. Brigadier-General of a Mixed Brigade in Fukien, 1913. Defence Commissioner in Fukien, 1914. Tuchun of Fukien, 1916. Civil Governor of Fukien. Expelled from Fukien as result of revolt in Oct. 1922. Recalled to Peking Jan. 1923 but never left Fukien. The post of Fukien Tuchun was done away with by this "recall" Jan. 21, 1923. Made Chuan Wei Chiangchun Nov. 1923. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI HSIEH-YANG, (李燮陽)** —Yunnan. Born 1881. Engineer. Graduate of Technical Department of Electricity and Railways in the Ohio University, U.S.A. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI K'AI-SHEN, (李開侁)** —Hupeh. Acting Chief Civil Administrator, Kwangtung, September 13, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan, May 1914. Chief Secretary to the President, 1917. Resident Commissioner in Urga. Retired. Commissioned to enforce Auti-Opium laws in Shensi and Hunan, Sept. 1922. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI KENG-YUAN (李根源)** .—Yunnan. Member of House of Representatives. Concerned in the "second revolution" of 1913. Joined Yunnan rebellion, 1915. Appointed Chief, Bureau of Aviation, Sept. 1922. Acting Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, Dec. 1922. Appointment confirmed Jan. 1923. First class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI KUO-CHEN, (李國珍)** —Kiangsi. Born 1881. A Graduate of a University in Japan. After returning from Japan was engaged as a Teacher in various Law Schools. Was instrumental in bringing about the Revolution in Kiangsi, and when the people there determined to kill Governor Feng, it was due to his speeches made before a great audience that the Governor was sent out of Kiangsi unmolested. Was a member of the Kiangsi Assembly and the late National Assembly. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan and a member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee, Chinputang. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce; of Education. 1916. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI KWEI-YUAN, (李奎元)** —Peking. Lieutenant-General. Commanding General of the 11th. Army Division. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LIEH-CHUN, (李烈鈞)** —Kiangsi. Studied six years in Japan. Tutuh of Kiangsi: dismissed and ordered to Peking, June 9, 1913. Deprived of all ranks and outlawed for his part in the rebellion, July 15, 1913. Reinstated as Tuchun of Kiangsi, 1916. Commander of Yunnan troops. Joined the Canton Government. First order of Merit. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI PING, (李鈁)** —Kansu. Born Feb. 17, 1887. Graduated from the Kansu Provincial College. President, Kansu Provincial Educational Association, since June, 1920. Kuomintang. Seventh class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI PING-SHU, (李秉恕)** —Fengtien. Born 1877. A Graduate of a Normal School in Tokio. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was a Teacher and Director of several Normal Schools. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1016 ([scan leaf 1067](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1067/mode/1up))

**LI SHENG-TO** (李盛鐸).—Kiangsi. Born 1862. Chinshih. Director, Peking University. Minister to Japan and Belgium. Magistrate of Peking. Special Commissioner to foreign countries to investigate politics. Governor of Shansi. Political Adviser to the President. Chairman of the Senate, dissolved after the Anfu defeat in 1920. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI SHI-HAO** (李思浩).—Chekiang. Born 1880. Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. Secretary in the Ministry of Finance in his early age, and concurrently rendered his service in the Salt Administration. Vice-Minister of Finance, 1916. Minister of Finance, 1919, Director-General of the Currency Reform Bureau. Director-General, Salt Administration. Member of the Anfu Political Club. Since the defeat of the Club he has been in hiding. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI SHIH-WEI** (李士偉).—Chihli. Member of the Tsanchengyuan. Resigned on becoming President of the Bank of China, 1915. appointed Minister of Finance, May 1921, but did not accept office. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI TING-HSIN** (李鼎新).—Fukien. Studied in England. Councillor, Ministry of Navy, September 5, 1912. Chief of Naval General Staff, December 11, 1912. Created Admiral (brevet), August 20, 1913. Commander-in-Chief of the Navy. Director of Kiangnan Arsenal. Cashiered of rank, but retained in office, December, 1915. Minister of Navy, May 1921 to date. Second order of Merit. 1st class Wenhu decoration. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI T'ING-YU** (李廷玉).—Acting Military Assistant Governor of Ulliassutai, August 24, 1912. Acting Deputy Commissioner of Occupation, Kiukiang, August 1, 1913. Commissioner of Occupation, South Kiangsi, Dec. 1913. Dismissed, August, 1914. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI TSAI-KENG** (李載賡).—Honan. Born 1883. Graduate of a Law School in Japan. Was Secretary of the Ministry of Communications and a member of the Advisory Council in Nanking Government. Was editor of Hsin Hwa Pao. Tientsin. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Author of The details of the Mines of Honan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI TSO-JEN** (李作人).—Shantung. Born 1850. Has been in business for over fifty years. President of Chefoo Y. M. C. A. since 1916. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI YAO-HSIANG** (Yew C. Lee), (李耀祥).—Kwangtung. Born 1882. Studied in Harvard University, U.S.A. Engineer, Peking-Kalgan, Canton-Hankow, and Chuchow-Chin-chow Railways, 1906-18. Surveyor, Chihli River Commission, 1918 to date. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI YING-CH'UAN** (李英銓).—Kwangtung. Born 1876. Graduate of Law School in Kwangtung. Was Director of a Normal School at Kwangtung; Delegate for prison reform at Wenyuenhsien; a practitioner in the local court of Kwangchow; Judge in Yuyuanhsien and Nanhsunghsien; and a lawyer in Kuangchow. Member of the Senate. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI YU-LIN** (李玉麟).—Special Commissioner for Disbanding troops in Anhwei, Nov., 1922. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI YUAN-HUNG** (黎元洪).—Hupeh. Born Oct. 19, 1864. Studied at Peiyang Naval College, graduating in 1888 after a course of six years. Served on a cruiser during the Sino-Japanese War. After the war he was engaged for service at Nanking by Viceroy Chang Chih Tung. On the latter's transfer to Wuchang he accompanied him to assist in the organization of the modern troops there. Thence he went to Japan for two years to study fortification. On his return he became a Major in the cavalry in 1895, and subsequently held several commands, including that of Colonel in the 21st Brigade. He was in charge of the organization of the Changteh Manoeuvres in 1905 and for the five follow- ing years served on the staff at Wuchang. On the outbreak of the Revolution at Wuchang he was forced into accepting the command of the revolutionary forces, whose operations he directed thenceforward. He was mainly instrumental in arranging for the Shanghai Peace Conference. After the abdication of the Manchus he was elected Vice-President of the Republic, and appointed Chief of the General Staff and Tutuh of Hupeh. (November, 1911). Given rank of General on September 7. Acting Tutuh, Kiangsi June 6, 1913. Re-elected Vice-President of the Republic. Oct. 7, 1913. On the death of Yuan Shih-kai, he became President of the Republic (June, 1916), resigned July 1, 1917, when Chang Hsun carried out his coup d'etat. When Hsu Shih-Chang left the capital June, 1922, Li was asked to as reassume the Presidency. Was compelled to leave the Capital, Sept. 1923 when plans were perfected for Tsao Kun to become President, Li first fled to Tientsin and later went to Shanghai and Japan. Author of various lecture notes not published. Formerly member of Chinputang but resigned therefrom when accepting Chief of General Staff. First Order of Merit. First class of Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1017 ([scan leaf 1068](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1068/mode/1up))

**LI YUNG-HAN, (李永瀚)** —Honan. Born Sept. 1859. Studied in a normal school in Japan. Vice-Chairman, Honan Provincial Assembly. Formerly of Kunghotang. — p. 1018 ([scan leaf 1069](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1069/mode/1up))

**LI YUAN-LIANG, (李元亮)** —Shantung. Born 1884. A Graduate of the High Normal School in Shantung. A member of the Shantung Provincial Assembly. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Teacher to several Schools in Shantung. Chinputang. — p. 1018 ([scan leaf 1069](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1069/mode/1up))

**LIANG CH'ENG-CHIU, (梁成久)** —Kwangtung. Born 1859. A Hsiutsai of the late Ching Dynasty. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Director and Teacher of several Schools, and is now one of the promoters of the Society for the Revival of Confucianism. Chinputang. Author of a poetical work in Chinese. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1018 ([scan leaf 1069](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1069/mode/1up))

**LIANG CH'I-CHAO, (梁啓超)** —Kwangtung. A brilliant scholar, who is best known as K'ang Yu-wei's most prominent disciple. Started the first Chinese daily paper in Peking, a small leaflet, containing an editorial only, which was given away gratuitously. Was proscribed, and had to fly for his life, after the coup d'état of 1898, and thereafter resided in Japan, where he conducted a paper in Chinese, devoted to the cause of reform. Like K'ang Yu-wei, advocated a limited monarchy in preference to a Republic. Returned to China after the Revolution, and then conducted a daily paper in Tientsin, advocating the spread of political education, and the diffusion of general knowledge among the Chinese. Appointed Vice-Minister of Justice in Yuan Shih-kai's first Cabinet, but refused to accept office. Editor of Yung Yen Pao ("Justice"), a bi-monthly periodical, and one of the chief organizers of the Chinputang. Minister of Justice in Hsiung Hsi-ling's Cabinet, September 11, 1913. Resigned 1914. Appointed Head of Currency Bureau. which was afterwards incorporated in the Ministry of Finance, November 15, 1912. Resides in Tientsin, and is a teacher of History in various colleges in North China. Visited Paris during the Peace Conference. — p. 1018 ([scan leaf 1069](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1069/mode/1up))

**LIANG FU. (梁宓)** —Fukien. Japan-educated. Was Presiding Judge, Procurator's Court for the Peking District. Appointed Chief Secretary to the Cabinet, Oct., 1922. Resigned Dec. 1922. — p. 1018 ([scan leaf 1069](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1069/mode/1up))

**Liang Ju-hao** (better known as M. T. Liang), (梁如浩).—Kwangtung. Educated in America. Taotai, Newchwang, April, 1906. Customs Taotai, Tientsin, April, 1907. Shanghai Taotai, October, 1907. Secretary of Board of Foreign Affairs, March, 1908. Chief Secretary to Government of Fengtien, July, 1908. Recalled to Peking, June, 1909. Vacated office, November, 1909. Offered post of Vice-Minister of Communications in Yuan Shih-kai's Cabinet of November 16, 1911, but did not accept. Nominated for the post of Minister of Communications in the first Republican Cabinet, but the nomination was rejected by the National Council. Elected Minister of Foreign Affairs on September 16, resigned November 15, 1912. Resides in Tientsin. Organized and was President of North China International Society of Famine Relief 1920-21. President, China International Famine Relief Commission. Adviser to the Chinese delegation to the Washington Conference, Nov. 1921. Was appointed Commissioner on April 26, 1922, by President Hsu to negotiate with the British Government on the question of return of Weihaiwei to China. First class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang Lai-k'uei** (L. K. Liang). (梁賚奎).—Fushan, Kwangtung. Born 1879. Studied at Peiyang University and Anglo-Chinese College at Tientsin. Studied Agriculture in Massachusetts Agriculture College, 1904-8; at Cornell University, 1908-10. B.S., M.S. Chinshih, 1910; Hanlin, 1911. Director, Experimental Station, Department of Agriculture, Government University, Peking, 1911-12. Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry, July 31, 1912. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Forestry, 1912-1913. In business since 1911. Author of Alkali Investigation; Wheat. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang, L. K** see Liang Lai K'uei. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang Lan-hsun** (梁瀾勳).—Kwangtung. Born 1871. Studied in the Queen's College, Hongkong. Associate of Arts Oxford University, Eng. Formerly Chinese Consul General at Melbourne, Australia. In 1920 became Superintendent of Customs, Kwangtung, and Commissioner of Customs for Kwangtung. Translated a certain book on the Science History of the 19th century. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang, M. T** see Liang Ju-hao. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang Pei** (梁培).—Kwangsi. Born 1880. A Graduate of the High Preparatory School at Kwangsi. Was a Teacher to several Schools. During the Revolution he assisted in the capture of the military depôt in Kwangsi. Chairman of Kuomintang branch at Nanning, and editor of the Minfengpao and Hsichiangpao of Kwangsi. Member of the Senate. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang Shang-tung** (梁上棟).—Secretary to the Ministry of War, 1915. Made a member in the College of Generals, Aug. 1922, Brigadier General in the Army. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang Sheng-te** (梁聲德).—Heilungkiang. Was a petty official during the Manchu regime. Became member of Heilungkiang Provincial Assembly during the first and second elections and is now Chairman of the Assembly. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**Liang Shih-yi** (梁士詒).—Kwangtung. Secretary to Tang Shao-yi on the occasion of his mission to India in 1906. Chief of Department of Board of Communications, 1907. Director of Railways in Board of Communications, 1907. Assistant Director of Chiao T'ung Bank, 1907. Senior Secretary of Board of Communications, July, 1909. Vacated appointments of Director of Railways and Assistant Director of Chiao T'ung Bank, February, 1911. Acting Vice-Minister of Communications, Novem- ber 1911. Acting Director of Imperial Chinese Posts, December, 1911. Acting Minister of Communications, January to March, 1912. Appointed Chief Secretary in the President's Office after the abdication of the Manchus (April, 1912). Acting Vice-Minister of Finance, May-September, 1913. Member of the Tsanchengyuan (Council of State). Director-General of the Shuiwuchu (Customs Administration), 1915. Director-General of the Bureau of Taxes, 1915. Resigned after failure of Yuan Shih-kai's monarchical project, of which he was one of the chief promoters. Speaker of the Senate May-October, 1918. Appointed, under influence of General Chang Tso-lin, Premier, Dec. 24, 1921, but was rejected by General Wu Pei-fu. Ordered to be arrested for trial, May 5, 1922, when Chang Tso-lin was defeated by the Chihli party. Visited Europe in 1924. — p. 1019 ([scan leaf 1070](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1070/mode/1up))

**LIANG YEN-FANG, (梁槐芳)** —Kwangtung. Born 1888. Graduated from the Department of Politics of the Kwangtung Law College. Presiding Judge, Higher Procurator's Court for Kwangtung since May 28, 1918. Fourth Class Wenhu Decoration. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIAO HSI-HSIEN, (廖希賢)** —Szechwan. Born 1885. A Graduate of Tung-wen College in Szechwan, and a University in Japan. Was editor of Szechwan Jih Pao, and he established a Finance College in Szechwan, which at present has more than 500 students. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Kunghotang. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIAO HSIEH, (廖燏)** —Honan. Born October 26, 1875. Studied in the First Normal School, Honan. Vice-Chairman, Honan Provincial Assembly. Formerly of Kuomintang. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN CHANG-MIN, (林長民)** —Fukien. Secretary to the Council of State, 1915. Minister of Justice, July, 1917. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN CHENG-HSUAN, (林正煊)** —Kwangtung. Born 1885. Graduated from the Kwangtung Provincial College. Chairman, Kwangtung Provincial Assembly since 1919. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN CHING-TING, (林煨廷)** —Appointed Tuli of Kiangsi, Jan. 1923. Concurrently Civil Governor, March, 1923. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN HAN, (林翰)** —Fukien. Born 1879. Graduated in political science from a college in Japan, 1911. Teacher to the Public Law School of Fukien. Vice-Chairman, Fukien Provincial Assembly, 1913. Chairman, Fukien Provincial Assembly. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN PAO-YI, (林葆惇)** —Fukien. Born 1862. Graduated from the Navigation Department of the Foochow Naval College. Commanding officer in the Pei Yang Navy Division. Admiral of the First Fleet of Chinese Navy. Minister of Navy in the Canton Military Government. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN PO-HO, (林伯和)** —Kwangtung. Born 1875. Established a Po-Ai Hospital in Chunhwahsien, and was Director for two years. Was four years Director of a Middle School in Au-men. In the time of Revolution he was appointed by the Tutuh of Kwangtung Pacificator of San Lo, and after two months he was made the Chief of General Department under the magistracy of Shuntehhsien. A member of the Y.M.C.A. Committee in Canton. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN SUNG-LING, (林松齡)** —Kirin. Born June 1868. Educated in the Ts'ung Wen Seminary, Kirin. Commissioner of Industries since July 1920. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN TA-LI, (林大閭)** —Chekiang. Born about 1883. Educated in Japan. Studied engineering. A Hanlin Compiler. Was locomotive engineer on the S.H.N. and Peking-Hankow railways. After doing some teaching in Peking, Ling entered the Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, and was Chief of the Mining Department. Appointed a Councillor, same Ministry, Sept. 4, 1922. Acting Vice-Minister, July 4, 1925. — p. 1020 ([scan leaf 1071](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1071/mode/1up))

**LIN TUNG-SHIH** (Lynn Tong-Sih, or Dun-Min T. S. Lynn).—(林桐實).—Fukien. Born 1881. Graduate, University of Paris. Attache to the Chinese Legation, Paris, 1902. Secretary, 1905-09. First Secretary of the Special Mission sent to Belgium, Denmark, Spain and Sweden, August 1909. First Secretary to the Chinese Legation, Paris, 1910. Charge d'Affaires, Paris, August, 1911. Charge d'Affaires in Portugal, France, Habana and Brazil. Adviser on Foreign Affairs to the President, 1918. Member of Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1918 to date. Second class Paokuang Chiaho and Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIN WEN-YUAN** (凌文淵 ).—Councillor in the Ministry of Finance Vice-Minister of Finance in Dr. Wang Chung-hui's Cabinet, 1922. When Premier Wang Ta-hsieh refused to assume his concurrent post of Minister of Finance, Lin was in charge of the Ministry till Feb. 1923. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LING PING** ( 凌冰).—Honan. Born July 14, 1894. A. B. Stanford University, 1916. M. A. Columbia University, 1917. Ph.D. Clark University, 1919. Dean of Nankai College. Tientsin, since 1919. Author of Public Schools and the War, and Outline of Psychology of the Children. Commissioner of Education for Honan, Sept., 1922. Resigned soon afterwards. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CH'ANG-PING** (劉長炳).—Yunnan. Acting Legal Commissioner, Sinkiang, December 16, 1912. Acting Head of Law Preparation Department, Sinkiang, January 19, 1913. Acting Taoyin in Sinkiang, Dec. 1917. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CHEN-HUA** (劉鎮華).—Honan. Born Oct. 1883. Graduated in the Law College, Peking. Civil Governor of Shensi since April 1, 1918. Also Commanding officer of the "Sung" Army. Acting Tuchun of Shensi, May 10, 1922. Made a full General in the Army, Dec. 1923. First class Tashou Chiaho and Second class Wenhu decorations. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CHIH-CHIEH** (劉之深).—Chihli. Japanese educated. Commissioner of Defence and Commander of 19th Army Corps, Kiangpeh, May, 1912. Commissioner of Occupation of Soochow, August 27, 1913. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CHIH-CHOW** (劉治洲).—Shensi. Born 1881. A Graduate of the Shanghai Li-hua College. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. After returning to his native place from Shanghai, he was engaged as Lecturer in several Schools. When his Province adopted the cause of Revolution, local "tufeis" arose and he trained a band of volunteers to suppress them, and he also held Fencheng against Sheng Yun for more than seventy days, until the inauguration of the Republic. Chenyuhui. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CHING-JEN** (劉鏡人).—Kiangsu. Minister to Holland, September, 1911. Minister to Russia, September, 1912. Appointed Minister to Japan, but did not proceed. President, Commission for Russian Affairs, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1922. Vice-Chairman, Committee of Foreign Affairs, May, 1923. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CHU-WU** (劉祖武).—Yunnan. General. Appointed Civil Governor of Yunnan on outbreak of revolt. 1915. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CHUAN-SHOU** (劉傳綬).—Fukien. Born 1870. Graduated in 1888 from the Peiyang Naval College, Tientsin. Was Councillor, and Department Head, Ministry of Navy. Vice-Minister of Navy. Counsellor of the Chiangchunfu, April 1918. Adviser to the Ministry of Navy. Nonpartisan. Fourth order of Merit. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1021 ([scan leaf 1072](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1072/mode/1up))

**LIU CHUNG-HUA, (劉鍾華)** —Yunnan. Born 1875. Graduated from Japanese College. Studied in two Normal schools in Tokio. Is teacher of Analytical chemistry in the Provincial Technical College and First Government Middle School. Vice-Chairman, Yunnan Provincial Educational Association, in 1911-1915. Re-Elected August 1919. Author and compiler of various lecture notes in chemistry. First Class Tashou Paokwang decoration. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU EN-YUAN, (劉恩源)** —Chihli. Studied in the Military Academy, Tientsin and later graduated from Germany. Member of the Ministry of War during Ching dynasty. Military Adviser to the President. Director of Pukow Port Administration, of Kiaochao port, etc. Appointed Minister of Finance and Chief of Salt Administration. Jan. 27. 1923. Relieved May 12, 1923. At the same time, he also became Chief of the Currency Bureau. Appointed Chairman, Commission for Adjustment of domestic and Foreign Loans, April, 1923. Fourth class of Merit. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU FU, (劉馥)** —Hunan. Born 1883. Educated in Japan. Compiler, Law Compiling Bureau. Nanking Provisional Government 1912. Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry, May, 1912. Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, Member of Political Conference. Examiner, Civil Service Examination, January, 1914. Councillor, Ministry of Interior, Member of Committee on Convocation of Parliament, Honorary Member of Financial Investigation Commission of the Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, Adviser to the Industrial Lottery Bureau, Director of Bureau of Port Development of Hankow, since January 1917. Vice-Minister of Interior, Nov. 1922 to Feb. 1923. Wrote several books on Salt administration, mining, politics, diplomacy, finance, etc. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU HSIEN-SHIH, (劉顯世)** —Kweichow. Chiangchun and Acting Civil Governor of Kweichow. Ordered to vacate his post, February, 1916. (Joined Yunnan revolt.) Commander-in-Chief of the Kweichow troops. Succeeded by General Lu T'ao. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU HUAI-SEN, (劉槐森)** —Anhwei. Acting Commissioner of Occupation of Kiukiang, August 4, 1913, of Kung Nan (South Kiangsi), Oct. 1913. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU HUAN-WEI, (W. W. Lau), (劉[illegible])** —Kwangtung. Born 1891. Graduated from Canton Christian College, 1909. Cornell University 1914 A.B., 1915 C.E. U. S. Army Field Engineer School, Fort Leavenworth. Graduated 1916. Member of faculty, Tsing Hua College, Peking. 1916-18. Engineer Surveyor, Chihli River Commission, Tientsin, 1918-19. Member of faculty, St. John's University, Shanghai, 1919-22. Asst. Professor, St. John's University, 1922. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU HUI-T'UNG, (劉會同)** —Kirin. Born April 10, 1888. Studied in the Kirin Normal College. Vice-Chairman, Kirin Provincial Assembly since September 1, 1918. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU JO-TSENG, (劉若曾)** —Chihli. Chinkiang Taotai, January, 1906. Proctor, Bureau of Government Administration, June, 1906. Vice-Director of High Court of Justice, November, 1906. High Commissioner Revision of Law March, 1911. Resigned May, 1912. Provincial Treasurer, Chihli, December, 1912. Acting Chief Civil Commissioner, Chihli, July 7, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan, Feb. 1914. — p. 1022 ([scan leaf 1073](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1073/mode/1up))

**LIU KUAN-HSIUNG** (劉冠雄).—Fukien. Educated at the Royal Naval College, Greenwich, and subsequently given a commission in the Chinese Navy. Distinguished himself at the Battle of the Yalu in the China-Japan war. Appointed Minister of the Navy in the first Republican Cabinet, which post he held for some years. Admiral Liu was a member of the National Council at Nanking, and also of the Southern Delegation which was sent to Peking to congratulate President Yuan Shih-kai upon his election. Acting Minister of Communications in July. Acting Minister of Education, January 13. Inspector-General of Southern Seas, August 14, 1913. Acting Tutuh of Fukien (additional). November, 1913. Minister of the Navy, July, 1917. Appointed Pacificator of Fukien. Nov. 1922, Commissioner of Defence for Fukien and Kwangtung Coast, April 3, 1923. Relieved of post, Nov. 26, 1923. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU KUEI-YI** (劉揆一).—Hunan. Educated in Japan, where he studied law and politics. Formerly edited several papers, and it was as a journalist that he achieved distinction during the Revolution. Minister of Commerce and Iindustry, August, 1912. Resigned July 18, 1913. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU LING-CHOU** (劉凌[illegible]).—Shensi. Born 1857. Vice-Chairman, Kansu Provincial Chamber of Commerce 1908-1909. Chairman since Sept. 1920. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU LIEN** (劉濂).—Kiangsi. Born 1875. Studied Law in Japan. Was Teacher to several Government and private Schools in his own Province. Was for six months Chief of the Department of Justice in Kiangsi. Chief of the Commission for the examination of judicial officials. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU MENG-TEN** (劉夢庚).--Chihli. Born 1882. Was Director-General of the Tientsin Mint. Appointed Governor of the Metropolitan District, May 19, 1922. 5th Order of Merit, 2nd class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU P'ENG-SHOU** (劉彭壽).--Chihli. Born 1875. Graduate of Chihli High Normal School. Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. Since the 33rd year of Kwang Hsu he was engaged as a Teacher in various High Schools in Chihli. Was a member of Red Cross Society during the Revolution; and established twelve Primary and High Schools in his native place during the past ten years. Member of Chihli Assembly, and President of the Committee of the House, June. 1912. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan Chinputang. Appointed Superintendent of the Tientsin Customs, March, 1922. Author of Outlines of Military History for Normal Schools, and Outlines of the History of the West. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU SHIH-HSUN** (劉式訓).—Kiangsu. Born 1868. Studied French for fifteen years both at Shanghai and Peking. Was Chinese Minister to France, and Acting Minister to Germany and Portugal. Member of the Arbitration Department of the Hague. President's Adviser on Foreign Affairs. Vice-Minister of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, January to August 8, 1913. Resigned. Special Commissioner Foreign Affairs, Shanghai. Vice-Minister of Foreign affairs, 1920. Resigned, Jan. 5, 1922. Vice-Chairman, Committee on Foreign Affairs, May, 1923. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU SHU-SHUN** (劉樹春).—Kirin. Born 1885. Studied in a native normal school. Vice-Chairman, Kirin Provincial Assembly since autumn, 1921. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**LIU TAO-JEN** (劉道仁).—Supervisor of the Changsha Customs and Special Delegate of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Hunan, 1915. — p. 1023 ([scan leaf 1074](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1074/mode/1up))

**Liu Tsun-hou, (劉存厚)** Szechwan. Born 1885. Graduated in Infantry Department of a Japanese Military College. Major General in the Army. Commanding General of the 2nd Army Division and concurrently Defence Commissioner of Chungking. Tuchun of Szechwan. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Liu Yu-lin, (Lew Yuk Lin), (劉玉麟)** Kwangtung. Born 1862. Graduated from Philips Academy Andover, Mass., U. S. A. LL. D. (Cantab Hon. causa) Consul-General in South Africa, October, 1904. Attached to Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1908. Chinese Representative at the International Opium Commission, Shanghai, January, 1909. Junior Councillor, Waiwupu, July, 1910. Minister to Great Britain, September, 1910-4. High Political Adviser to the Military Government. Salt Commissioner for Kwangtung and Kwangsi. Second class Double Dragon Medal, Tashou Chiaho, First class Wenhu Decoration. Received also decorations from Belgium, Portugal, Italy, Russia and Germany. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Liu Yuan, (劉垣)** Chihli. For sometime Manager, Tientsin Branch of the Bank of Communications. Was in banking business in Peking. Vice-Minister of Commerce, October 25, 1913. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Lo Ch'ao-han, (羅朝潼)** Studied in the Peiyang Telegraph School. Director, Chinese Government Telephone Administration, Peking Office, since April, 1912. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Lo Chia-heng, (羅家衡)** Kiangsi. Born 1883. Graduate of a University in Japan. Established a Law School in Kiangsi and was teacher there. When the Revolution broke out in Wuchang, was appointed Chief of the Bureau of Civil Affairs, Kiangsi. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Appointed Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, Aug. 22, 1922. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Lo P'an-hui, (羅泮輝)** Kwangtung. Born 1881. Graduate of Tientsin University; of Harvard University and Chicago University, U.S.A. Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Kwangtung, April 25, 1913. In 1911 during the Revolution he was appointed an official of the Legislative Bureau and Vice-Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs, Kwangtung, and then Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs. Kuomintang. Author of The Laws of China in English. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Lo Pei-chin, (羅佩金)** Yunnan. Born 1879. A Graduate of the Military Officers' Training School. Major-General of the Army. While in Japan he edited a periodical called Yunnan Cha Chih, which promulgated revolutionary doctrines. Invited to Yunnan by Viceroy Li Ching-hsi, and given various appointments. Before the Revolution was appointed Brigadier of the 74th Brigade. By the help of General Tsai Ao the whole Province was captured and troubles from local "tufeis" suppressed. Was appointed Military Delegate to Peking, and Military Councillor of the President's Office. Chief Civil Administrator of Yunnan, January, 1913; resigned April 4, 1913. Tuchun of Szechwan, July, 1916. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Lo Tsung-yi, (羅忠詒)** Fukien. Born 1886. Cambridge University; B.A. (Economics Tripos). A Chin-shih and Hanlin Compiler of the late Dynasty. English Secretary to the Governor of Kwangsi, 1909. Joined Waiwupu, 1910. Transferred to the Chiaotungpu on the establishment of the Republic. Director of the College of Communications, 1912. Secretary in the President's Office and Member of the Office of the Master of Ceremonies, 1913. First Secretary of the Chinese Legation in London, July, 1914. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Lo Wen-kan, (羅文幹)** Kwangtung. Born 1888. Studied law in England, M.A. Oxon, and member of Inner Temple. Judicial Commissioner for Kwangtung, 1911-1912. Procurator-General, 1912-1915. Vice-President, Law Codification Commission since 1916. Professor of Law, Peking Government University and School for Judicial Officers. Appointed Vice-Minister of Justice, Dec. 29, 1922. In charge of the Ministry, April 22, 1922. Appointed Chief Judge of the Supreme Court, June 15, 1922, Chief of Salt Administration and of Currency Bureau, Sept. 1922.
