Minister of Finance, Sept. to Dec. 1922, when he was arrested and for many months detained on charges of which he was found innocent. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 1024 ([scan leaf 1075](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1075/mode/1up))

**Loe Meng Len, Dr** see Jao Meng-jen. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Long, K. T** see Lang Kuo-chen. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Loo, Hing-yun, (H. Y.)** see Lu Hsing-yuan. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lou Tseng Tsiang** see Lu Cheng-hsiang. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lu Ch'ang-yu** (陸長佑).—Kiangsu. Born Nov. 12, 1862. Commissioner of Administration for Chihli since July 1917. Second class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho and Fourth class Wenhu decorations. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lu Cheng-hsiang** (Lou Tseng Tsiang), (陸徵祥).—Kiangsu. Born 1871. Educated in the Shanghai Language School and the Tung Wen Kuan, Peking. Interpreter of the Chinese Legation at St. Petersburg, 1890. Attaché, 1892. Secretary, 1895. Deputed to accompany the Chinese Envoy Extraordinary to the Coronation of the late Tsar. Chinese Delegate at the Hague Conference in 1899. Minister to the Netherlands, 1905. Delegate at the Second Hague Conference in 1907. Minister to Holland (renewed) in 1908. Negotiated the Consular Convention with Holland. Sent to the Hague to exchange ratifications of the Convention in 1911, and thence proceeded to St. Petersburg to undertake negotiations with the Russian Government for the revision of the Treaty of 1881. Appointed Minister to Russia. Elected Minister of Foreign Affairs in the first Republican Cabinet, and arrived at Peking on May 24th 1912 to take up the post. When Tang Shao-yi, the Premier, was granted sick leave, Lu was appointed acting premier, and on the resignation of Tang, was elected Premier but retained the portfolio of Foreign Affairs. Was on sick leave during August and September and resigned Premiership on September 23. On the resignation of Liang Juhao, he was appointed Minister of Foreign Affairs, November 15, 1912. Resigned September 4, 1913. Master of Ceremonies at President Yuan's Inauguration. Acting Secretary of State and Minister of Foreign Affairs, 1915. Chief Delegate to the Paris Peace Conference. President, National Famine Prevention Commission since May 19, 1921. Appointed Minister to Switzerland, June 15, 1922. Non-partisan. Chiaho Pao-kuang Chiaho and Second class Wenhu decorations. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lu Chin** (陸錦).—Chihli. Born 1880. Studied Military Science in a Japanese School. Was connected with the Army in Chihli and Shantung before he became Assistant-Chief of the General Staff and Commanding General of the 9th. Army Division till Mar. 1924. Made a full general in the Army, Nov. 1923, Minister of War to succeed Chang Shao-tseng, Jan. 12, 1924. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lu Chin-shan** ([illegible]金山).—Appointed Commander 18th Division, August 31, 1921. Hupeh Commander of the 3rd and 4th Mixed Brigade. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lu Chun** (呂均).—Appointed Chief Secretary of Cabinet Office, Jan. 4, 1923. Relieved May 7, 1923. Brigadier General in the Army. Member, Commission for Adjutment of Domestic and Foreign Loans. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lu Hai-huan** (呂海寰).—Shantung. Minister to Germany, June, 1897. President of Censorate, August, 1901. Minister of Foreign Affairs. November, 1901. Commissioner for Treaty Revision, 1902. President of Board of War, January, 1906. Again Minister of Foreign Affairs, June, 1907. President of Revenue Council, Sept. 1907. Director-General of Tientsin Pukow Railway, January, 1908, from which post he wa removed in July, 1909. Placed in charge of Red Cross work, by Imperial Decree, during the Revolution, an appointment which was confirmed by the President in September, 1912. President, Shantung Famine Relief Society, 1920-21. — p. 1025 ([scan leaf 1076](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1076/mode/1up))

**Lu Hsiao-chih, (呂孝植)** —Shensi. Born May 3, 1883. Studied Agriculture in Japan. Chujen. Appointed Commissioner of Education for Hupeh, July, 1919. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Hsing-lin, (陸杏林)** —Kwangsi. Born 1880. Studied Local Administration in Kwangsi. Member, Tzu Yi Chü, Kwangsi, 1909. Ch'ing Lien Taoyin since October, 1915. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Hsing-yuan, (Hing-yun Loo or H. Y. Loo), (盧興原)** —Macao. Born 1885. After studying Chinese classics he went to England in 1909 under private scholarship. He stayed in England for seven years and attended Oxford University and the Inner Temple in London. He learned the French Language in France for about a year. He was called to the English Bar in 1916 and took his M.A. Degree at Oxford in 1915. Since he returned to China he has been practising law in Shanghai and Canton. Was a lecturer on law, Soochow University and has on two occasions acted as Departmental Chief of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in the Military Government of Canton. President of the Euro-American Returned Students' Association, Adviser to the Civil Governor of Kwangtung and a Compiler in the Canton Law Commission in 1920. Appointed Chief Justice of the Supreme Court at Canton Feb. 1921. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Kung-wang, (呂公望)** —Chekiang. Born 1880. Graduated from the Army Officers' Training School, Paoting. Was a training officer in Chekiang for a number of years after graduation. During the Revolution of 1911, was Chief of Staff, Chekiang Revolutionary Army. Became General of the Chekiang 11th Brigade in 1912. In June of the same year, was appointed commanding Officer of the 6th. Division. One month later, was appointed Defence Commissioner of the Kashing-Wuchow district, Che. Appointed Tuchun and Civil Governor of Chekiang, March, 1916. Commander-in-Chief, Chekiang Provincial Army, July, 1918. Became Chief of Staff, Canton Military Government in April, 1920. Second Order of Merit, First class Tashou Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Pi, ([illegible])** —Hupeh. Secretary Department of Civil Service, Cabinet, August 17, 1912. Secretary to Cabinet, October 18, 1912. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Pu-o, (盧步峨)** —Shansi. Born 1877. Vice-Chairman, Shansi Provincial Chamber of Commerce. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Tao, (盧燾)** —Kwangsi. Commanding Officer of the 2nd Brigade of the First Army Division of Kweichow. Succeeded General Liu Hsien-shih as Commander-in-Chief of Kweichow troops, 1920. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Tiao-yuan, (呂調元)** —Anhwei. Chinshih. Was magistrate of Tientsin. Governor of Hupeh. Governor of Shensi, 1915. Civil Governor of Anhwei, 1920. Again in 1923. Relieved, Dec. same year. Was specially cited for Merit by the President, April, 1923, in connection with the disbandment of soldiers in Anhwei. Commissioner for Promotion of Railway System—Anhwei, Dec. 1923. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Ting, (陸定)** —Kiangsu. Born 1873. Studied in Japan. Was Chief of the Audit Department in the Ministry of Finance, Chief Secretary of Tzu Cheng Yuan, and is Councillor to the Ministry of Finance and member of several Committees of the Ministry. Kuomintang. Author of Banking System, etc. — p. 1026 ([scan leaf 1077](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1077/mode/1up))

**Lu Tsung-yu, (陸宗輿)** —Chekiang. Born 1875. A Graduate of a University in Japan, and was appointed to accompany the Commissioners to foreign countries to study political affairs. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan, and Chairman of the Committee of the Bank of Communications. Was a Teacher in the Government University in Peking, 1902; Chief Secretary in Chungwenmen Octroi, 1903; studied politics in foreign countries, 1904; a member of the Tzu Cheng Yuan, 1909; Vice-President of the Board of Finance, 1911. Chinputang. Author of The Currency, etc. Minister plenipotentiary to Japan, 1915. Manager-in-Chief of the Currency System Bureau. Was forced to retire from official life by strong public sentiment against the so-called pro-Japanese officials in May, 1919. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Lu Yung-hsiang, (盧永祥)** —Shantung. Born 1867. Educated in the Military School, Shanhaikwan. Military Commissioner of Shanghai and Woosung, January 6, 1917. Tuchun of Chekiang, August 16, 1919. Abolished his Tuchunship himself and became Director of Military Affairs of Chekiang, June 1922. First Order of Merit. Tashou decoration. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Lu Yung-ting, (陸榮廷)** —Kwangsi. Born 1856. At one time a leader of bandits, but reclaimed and took military service under Government. Commander of an expedition under Viceroy Tsen Chun-hsuan against bandits in Kwangsi. Formerly a Brigade-General of the troops on the left bank of the West River. Tutuh of Kwangsi, 1914, afterwards Chiangchun. In the Civil War of 1917-18 joined the South and became Minister of War in the Canton Government. Inspecting General of Liang Kwang. Was driven out of Kwangsi by Chen Chiung-ming, 1921. Was appointed High Defence Commissioner for Kwangsi Frontier Nov., 1922. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Lung Chi-kuang, (龍濟光)** —Yunnan. Tso-Chiang Tao, Kwangsi, January, 1908. Commander-in-Chief, Kwangsi, March, 1909. Pacificator, Kwangtung, July 26, 1913. Tutuh and Acting Chief Civil Administrator, Kwangtung, August 3, 1913. Additionally to Command the Water Police, and Ships of War on the West River and coasts of Kwangtung, August 22, 1913. Chiangchun, Kwangtung, 1914. Field-Marshal. Created Duke of the First Order, December, 1915. Created Prince of the second degree for services in Yunnan, January, 1916. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Lung Chien-chang, (龍建章)** —Kwangtung. Born 1872. Councillor, Ministry of Communications, May, 1912. Civil Governor of Kweichow, 1914; cashiered January, 1916. Minister of Communications, 1917. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Lung Min-hsiu, (龍敏修)** —Honan. Born 1877. Graduated from a three year course in the Police Officers' Training School, Tientsin. Commissioner of Police, Honan, since Nov. 1918. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Lynn, Dun-Min T. S** see Lin Tung-shih. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Lynn Tong-sih** see Lin Tung-shih. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Ma Chen-pin, (馬振濱)** —Taoyin of the Liukiang Circuit in Kwangsi, 1915. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Ma An-liang, (馬安良)** —Kansu. Born 1853. Mohammedan. Brigade-General, Iii, October, 1903. Commander-in-Chief, Kansu, April, 1912. Second class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**Ma Chin-hsu, (馬金敘)** —Anhwei. Formerly Brigade-General in several places. Commander-in-Chief, Kansu, February, 1912. Commander-in-Chief, Chihli, April, 1912. — p. 1027 ([scan leaf 1078](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1078/mode/1up))

**MA CHUN, (馬駿)** —Shansi. Born February 4, 1882. Studied at University of Shansi, M.A., and Imperial College, London. Inspecting Commissioner for Hotung, 1913. Supervisor General, Eighth Mining District, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, 1914. Member of Parliament. Hotung Taoyin, Shansi, since May 22, 1918. Was member of Mintang; now non-partisan. Third class Chiaho and Fifth Wenhu decorations. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA FU-HSIANG, (馬福祥)** —Kansu. Brigade-General, Palikun, New Dominion, March, 1909. Acting Chief Executive Officer, Kokonor, July-August, 1912. Commander of Guards Division, Altai, October 10, 1912. Was Military Commissioner of Ninghsia, Kansu. Appointed Tartar General of Suiyuan, 1921. First Class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA HSIAO-CHIN, (馬小進)** —Kwangtung. Born 1887. Studied in the following schools: School of Political Science, Canton; St. Stephen's College, Hongkong; Columbia University, U.S.A., and New York University, U.S.A. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was an officer of the Southern Branch of Tung Meng Hui eight years ago. Was editor of several papers and periodicals, and has been Peking correspondent to several papers and periodicals. Kuomintang. Author of The Diary of a Travel, The Literature of the World, The Diary of Shiaocheng, The Latest General Outline of Finances, Liberty of the Citizens, and The Suffragists. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA HSU-LUAN, (馬敘倫)** —Chekiang. Born 1885. Studied in the Hangchow Middle School. Later became teacher in the same school and in various schools and colleges in Chekiang, Canton, Peking, etc. Was at one time, President of the First Normal School of Chekiang. Appointed Commissioner of Education, same province, July, 1922 and Vice-Minister of Education, Oct., 1922. Member of Commission for Readjustment of National Finance till Aug. 1923. Author of three works on Chinese literature. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA, K. S** see Ma Kuo-chi. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA KUO-CHI, (K. S. Ma), (馬國驥)** —Kiangsu. Graduate, St. John's University, Shanghai; and Harvard University and Columbia University, U.S.A. Dean, Wuchang Teachers' College, 1917. Professor, Tsing Hua College, 1917-20. Member, Secretaries' Office of the President. Member, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA LIANG, (馬良)** —Chihli. Born 1876. Lieutenant-General, Defence Commissioner of Tsinan, Shantung, and Commander of the 2nd Division of the Frontier Defence Troops, till 1920. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA LIANG, (馬良)** —Kiangsu. Born 1840. Acting President of Peking Government University, October 18, 1912; resigned, December 27, 1912. Appointed one of President's nominees on Political Council, November, 1913. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA LIEN-CHIA, (馬聯甲)** —Lt.-General in the Army. Commanding General of the first Anhwei Army Division and Defence Commissioner of Southern Anhwei. Appointed Assistant to the Tuchun of Anhwei on the removal of Ni Ssu Chung. 1920. Was ordered to attend to the Military affairs in Anhwei upon the abolishment of the Tuchun in that province, Oct. 7, 1922. Was specially cited for Merit by the President, April, 1923, in connection with the disbandment of Soldiers in Anhwei. Was given concurrent post as Civil Governor of Anhwei, Dec. 1923. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA LIN-YI, (馬鄰翼)** —Honan. Born 1874. Chujen. Was interested in education in his native province, having been the Head of Paoching (Honan) Educational Association, Superintendent of Honan Middle School, of Railway School, etc. Later he joined the Board of Education as its Councillor. Member of Commission for Preparation of Con- stitutional Government. Was sent to Kansu as Commissioner of Education for that Province. There he became successively Commissioner of Industries, of Finance, Taoyin of P'inglang, etc. Was Commissioner of Education for Anhwei till 1920 when he became the Commissioner of Education for Chihli. Promoted to Vice-Minister of Education, May, 1921. Resigned, Dec. 27, 1921. — p. 1028 ([scan leaf 1079](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1079/mode/1up))

**MA LUNG-PIAO** (馬龍標).—Shantung. Lieutenant-General, Mongol Red Banner, January, 1911. Commander of 5th Division, Assistant Commissioner for Defence of Shantung, March, 1912. Inspector General of Peking December, 1917 to June, 1923. Third Order of Merit. — p. 1029 ([scan leaf 1080](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1080/mode/1up))

**MA TSUN-FA** (馬存發).—Yunnan. Military Governor of the Chaochow Circuit in Kwangtung, 1915.—Lieut. General in the Army. Brigade General Kwangtung First Mixed Brigada. Age 57. — p. 1029 ([scan leaf 1080](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1080/mode/1up))

**MA YIN-CH'U** (馬寅初).—Chekiang. Born 1884. Studied in Peiyang University, Graduated from Yale University, U.S.A. 1910. M.A. Graduated from Columbia University, 1914. Ph.D. Professor, Peking Government University. Became its Dean. Dean of Commercial College, Shanghai. Advisor to the Chekiang Hsing Yeh Bank. Special member, Bank of China. — p. 1029 ([scan leaf 1080](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1080/mode/1up))

**MAI YING-CHUN** (Y. D. Mark), (麥英俊).—Kwangtung. Born Aug 21, 1892. Graduated in Law from Purdue University (U.S.A.) 1917 and in Commerce from University of Chicago, 1919. B.A. Deputy Chief, Bureau of Foreign Affairs, Kwangtung, from Aug. 1920 to May 1921. Appointed Superintendent of Wuchow Customs and concurrently Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Kwangsi. — p. 1029 ([scan leaf 1080](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1080/mode/1up))

**MARK, Y. D** see Mai Ying-chun. — p. 1029 ([scan leaf 1080](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1080/mode/1up))

**MEI HUA-CHUEN** (梅華銓).—Born in U.S.A., 1888. Studied in Kwangtung, 1890-95; Hongkong, 1895-96; San Francisco, 1896-98; and New York, 1898-1904. College of the City of New York, 1904-08; and Columbia University, 1908-11. B.Sc. LL.B. M.A. Phi Beta Kappa. Assistant Manager, Chinese Students' Monthly, 1909-11. Graduate law course, New York University, 1913-14, Juris Doctor, June, 1914. Law clerkship in Wall Street firm, 1914-15. Court interpreter, New York Courts, 1910-15. Writer on Chinese affairs, New York journals, 1909-15. English Secretary, Tung Meng Hui, New York branch, 1911-15. Secretary and Director, The China Society of America, Inc., New York, 1912-15. Admitted to bar of New York Supreme Court, also bars of United States District Court, and United States Circuit Court of Appeals, 1915. Returned to China, Sept. 1915. Secretary, National Committee, Y.M.C.A.'s of China, and also Editor-in-chief, China's Young Men, 1915-16. Instructor in a law school, Shanghai, 1915 to date. Office Manager and editor, Intelligence Bureau, Republican Government of China, 1916. Admitted to Shanghai American Bar and Mixed Court Bar, 1916. Official Reporter, United States Court for China, 1916-17. Director, Chinese Y.M.C.A., 1917 to date, its Vice-Chairman, 1918-19. Practising attorney and Counsel-at-law, Shanghai, 1917 to date. Legal Adviser to Commissioner of Foreign Affairs, Shanghai, 1917-18; to Administration of Sequestered German and Austrian Vessels, Shanghai, 1918. Trustee, Cantonese Union Church, Shanghai, 1918-20. Director, Yangtsepoo Social Centre, 1918 to date. Secretary, Columbia Alumni Association of China, 1918-20, Chairman, 1920-22. Executive Committeeman, American University Club, 1917-18. Committeeman, Society for Constructive Endeavour, Shanghai, 1918. One of organizers and directors of International Anti-Opium Association, 1919-22. Director, Intelligence Bureau, Southern Peace Delegation, Shanghai, 1919. Member: Far Eastern American Bar Association, American Returned Students' Club, American University Club. Adviser, China Bureau of Public Information, Shanghai, 1919 to date. Author of various articles published in magazines in China and abroad. — p. 1029 ([scan leaf 1080](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1080/mode/1up))

**MEI KUANG-HSI, (梅光羲)** .—Kiangsi. Born 1878. Graduate of a University in Japan, and Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. Started various colleges at Hupeh; introduced the system of Procuratorate and Prison Reform in Hupeh and Kwangtung; started the Library in the Government University at Peking; and established the Society for the Investigation of Buddhism at Hupeh and the Buddhist Society at Peking. Is the author of Outlines of Buddhist Doctrines. Chief of the Department of Navigation of the Ministry of Communications, 1914. Member, Civil Court of Equity. Superintendent of Telegraphs at Tsinan. Departmental Chief, Bureau of Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**MENG EN-YUAN, (孟恩遠)** .—Chihli. Born 1855. Military Governor of Kirin, 1915. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**MI FENG-CHI, (米逢吉)** .—Chihli. Born 1882. After attending school at home, entered a university in Japan in 1904, studied Political Economy, graduating in 1911. Started a Model Farm in his native district before going to Japan. Was interested in local government and public welfare affairs since his return to China. Elected member of Chihli Provincial Assembly in 1912, and became its Vice-Chairman in 1921. During 1913-1916, Mi promoted an Agricultural Colonization Society, known as the Ping Feng Shih, with a capital of $40,000 and 20,000 mu of land; this Society introduced agriculture in Suiyuan and West Mongolia. Organized the Hopei Daily News, 1919, and became its Editor. Author of various articles published in the News. Kuomintang. 2nd Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**MIAO CHING-KUEI, (苗繹剡)** .—Chihli. Born December 14, 1859. Vice-Chairman, Kirin Chamber of Commerce since 1913. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**MO YUNG-HSIN, (莫榮新)** .—Kwangsi. Promoted from a common soldier. Defence Commissioner in Kwangsi and Kwangtung. With the support of General Lu Yung-ting, Mo was made Tuchun of Kwangtung. Was driven back to Kwangsi by General Chen Chiung-ming in 1920. Third Order of Merit. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**MOU LIN, (牟琳)** .—Kweichow. Born 1877. Graduate of a Normal School in Japan, and a Chujen of the late Dynasty. In the 1st year of Hsuan Tung he was elected member of the Tzu Yi Chu of Kweichow, and a member of Tzu Cheng Yuan. Established more than 100 primary schools. Chinputang. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**MU OU-CH'U, (穆藕初)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1876. American educated. Studied in the University of Wisconsin and in an agricultural school in Texas, U.S.A. M.A. Manager of two leading spinning mills in Shanghai and of one in Chenghsien, Honan. Director, Shanghai Chamber of Commerce and of Industrial Bank. President, Committee on Cotton Culture of the Spinning Millers' Guild and of Cotton Exchange, Shanghai. Author of the books on industry. Adviser to the President and to the Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**NA TUNG, (那桐)** .—Manchu. Sub-Chancellor, Grand Secretariat, December, 1899. Minister, Tsung-li Yamen, June, 1900. Junior Vice-President, Board of Revenue, November, 1900. Special Embassy to Japan, July, 1901, when he ceased to be Minister of Tsung-li Yamen. Minister, Board of Foreign Affairs, February, 1902. President, Board of Revenue, June, 1903. President, Board of Foreign Affairs, and member of Finance Committee, November, 1903 Grand Secretary, January, 1906. Member Revenue Council, January, 1918. Inspector-General of Troops, November, 1908. Junior Guardian of Heir-Apparent, December, 1908. Grand Councillor, January, 1909. Acting Viceroy of Chihli, June, 1909. Appointed Vice-Premier on the formation of Prince Ching's Cabinet in May, 1911. Relieved of office, and made Vice-President of the Privy Council on November 1, 1911. Retired into private life during the latter stages of the Revolution, and lived at Tsingtao. — p. 1030 ([scan leaf 1081](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1081/mode/1up))

**NA YEN-T'U (Prince No), (那彥圖)** —Mongol. A Prince of the First Order, and Jassak of the Khalka Mongols. Superintendent of Customs at Peking, September, 1896. General Chamberlain, November, 1903. Lieutenant-General of Bordered Yellow Banner, 1903. One of the Mongolian members of the National Council and of the Kung-ho-tang, and Tartar General of Tarbagatai, 1914. Appointed Commissioner of Pacification to Mongolia, Sept. 1922. Made a full general in the Army Nov. 27, 1923. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**NAN KUEI-HSING, (南桂馨)** —Shansi. Born 1883. After graduation from the Imperial Shansi University, Nan went to Japan for further education, graduating from a Police Officers' Training School there. Sian Superintendent of Police and Commissioner of Police for Shansi since June, 1917. Second class Wenhu and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**NAN YAO-CHUN, (南岳峻)** —Shensi. Born 1883. Graduated from a law school in Peking and in political science from a Japanese University. Was Intendant of Police in his own province before he was elected Chairman of Shensi Provincial Assembly. 4th class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**NI SSU-CH'UNG, (倪嗣沖)** —Anhwei. Born 1871. Acting Commissioner for Internal Affairs, Heilungkiang, August, 1908; substantive, April, 1909; degraded, December, 1909. Provincial Treasurer, Honan, December, 1911; relieved, July 21, 1912. Appointed to reorganize Honan Frontier Affairs, July 5, 1912. Occupation Commissioner, Huanpei (Anhwei Northern Circuit), July 22, 1913; additionally, Huanpei, Intendant, July 22, 1913. Tutuh of Anhwei, and additionally Acting Civil Administrator, July 27, 1913. Field-Marshal, 1915. Created Duke of the First Order, December, 1915. Remained in Anhwei as Tuchun; resigned October, 1920. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**NIEH HSIEN-FAN, (聶憲藩)** —Anhwei. Educated in Japan. Was Defence Commissioner of Chefoo. Civil Governor of Anhwei. Appointed Commander of the Peking Gendarmerie, May 28, 1922. Chiangchung, Oct. 1922. Made of full general, the Army, Nov. 1923. First class Wenhu decoration. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**NIU CH'UAN-SHAN, (鈕傳善)** —Born 1877. Was a magistrate and later a Taotai in Ching Dynasty. Acting Civil Governor for Shensi, 1914. Director of the Sale of Tobacco and Wines, 1915-1916. Appointed Vice-Minister of Finance, June 1, 1921. Resigned, Nov. 6, 1921. Was President of the defunct Yu Feng Bank. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**NIU YUNG-CHIEN, (鈕永建)** —Kiangsu. Born 1873. Studied in Hupeh Military Academy and in Japan and Germany. Assistant Chief of General Staff, Nanking Military Government. Was Military representative stationed at Shanghai. Councillor to President. April 19, 1912. Joined the rebellion of 1913 and outlawed. Visited America 1914. Appointed President of Commission on Mongolian Frontier Affairs 1923 but released from that post shortly afterward. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**OU-YANG CHEN-SHENG, (歐陽振聲)** —Hunan. Born 1882. Studied in the Wen Pu Tung School Hupeh and graduated in Political Economy in the Waseda University, Japan. Delegate from Hunan in the Provisional Parliament of Nanking, 1911. Member of Senate in the Provisional Parliament of 1912. Member of House of Representatives, 1913. Secretary, Military Government in Canton, August 23, 1918, Vice-Minister of the Canton Military Government, since May 27, 1920. Author-compiler of History of Chinese Philosophy (in printing). Member of Kuo Min Tang, 1912; of Cheng Hsueh Hui, 1916. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1031 ([scan leaf 1082](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1082/mode/1up))

**OU-YANG P'U-TS'UN, (歐陽溥存)** —Kiangsi. Graduated in political science from a Japanese University. Was sometime member of Ministry of Interior and is a Taoyin in Kansu. Author of two Chinese dictionaries published in Shanghai. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**OU-YANG WU, (歐陽武)** .—Kiangsi. Born 1878. Defence Commissioner, Kiukiang. Dismissed and degraded, July 31, 1913. Brought to Peking for trial, and sentenced to eight years imprisonment. Specially pardoned by the President. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'A LO-TA, (帕勒塔)** .—Sinkiang. A Mongolian prince. Studied in Japan. General Administrator of Altai, May 1912. Member of Senate, 1917. Member of Chiangchunfu. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'AN CHU-YING, (潘矩楹)** .—Shantung. Born 1877. Graduated from an Army Officers' Training School in Japan. Member of Chiangchunfu. Brigadier-General in the army. Was at different times, Director of Aeronautic Bureau. Chiangchun of Suiyuan. Deputy Tutung of Kwei-hua, Tutung of Suiyuan. Military Adviser to the President, to the Inspecting-Generals of Chihli, Shantung and Honan; and of Manchuria. Commander of the 20th Army Division. Second class Chiaho, Wenhu and Tashou Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'AN FU, (潘復)** .—Shantung. Born 1871. Chujen. Industrial Taotai of his own province, 1911. Appointed Chief of Industrial Bureau, 1913. Organized Lufeng Spinning Mill in Tsinan, Autumn, 1915. Shortly afterwards, he was appointed Associate Director of National Conservancy Bureau. In December, 1918, he was appointed Associate Director, Grand Canal Improvement Board. Vice-Minister of Finance, December 1919. Concurrently Director of National Salt Administration. Resigned Nov. 5, 1921. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'AN HSUEH-HAI, (潘學海)** .—Kiangsi. Born 1876. A Graduate of a University in Japan. Chief of the Kiangsi Bureau of Legislature for one year, after the Revolution, and member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Non-partisan. Translated into Chinese a Japanese book on The Constitution of the Japanese Empire. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'AN LING-KAO, (潘齡皋)** .—Chihli. Hanlin of Ching Dynasty, Magistrate in Kansu. Held various positions in Shensi and Kansu. Commissioner of Finance, Kansu. Taoyin and Customs Superintendent in Kansu. Appointed Civil Governor of Kansu, Oct. 6, 1921. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'AN TSU-YI, (潘祖彝)** .—Fukien. Born 1882. Graduate of Fukien High School, and a returned student from Japan. In 1912, editor of the Chung Pao in Fukien, but the paper was closed by force by Pan Shiao-sung. In the same year was elected member of the Fukien Assembly, and a member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Is connected with several commercial enterprises, such as tea, paper, silk, and medicine. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'AN WAN-CH'AO, (潘萬超)** .—Heilungkiang. Graduate of middle school in his native province. Is new Vice-Chairman of Heilungkiang Provincial Assembly. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**PAO KWEI-CHING, (鮑貴卿)** .—Fengtien. General. Graduated from the Kai Ping Military Academy. Appointed Defence Commissioner of Tatung, Anhwei, 1913. Director, National Military Training Institution. Appointed Military Governor of Heilungkiang, July, 1917, and concurrently appointed to act as Civil Governor. Military Governor of Kirin, 1919. Director-General Chinese Eastern Railway. Appointed Minister of War, Dec. 25, 1921. Resigned, June 11, 1922. — p. 1032 ([scan leaf 1083](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1083/mode/1up))

**P'EI CH'I-HSUN** (裴其勳).—Honan. Graduate of Peiyang Military Academy. Military tutor of Shantung Army. Defence Commissioner of Kirin and Changchun, 1915. General First Mixed Brigade. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**PI KUEI-FANG** (畢桂芳).—Peking. Born 1865. Studied Russian Language in Peking. Councillor at Tarbagatai, May, 1912. Tuchun and Civil Governor of Heilungkiang, 1916. Tutuh and Chief Civil Administrator, Heilungkiang, July 16, 1913. Promoted full General of Manchu Banner Forces, November 28, 1913. High Military Adviser to the President. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**PIEN CHIH-CHING** see Pien Shou-ching. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**PIEN FU-SUN** (F. S. Bien), (卞福孫).—Kiangsu. Born 1887. Studied in Brown University, U.S.A. Judge, Peking Local Court, 1909-11. Manager, Bank of China, Antung Branch, Sept. 1917. Transferred to Pengpu, Jan. 1919. Sub-Manager, Mukden Branch, Oct. 1919. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**PIEN SHOU-CHING** (邊守靖 (Alias PIEN CHIH-CHING 邊深濟).—Chihli. Born 1884. A graduate of the Law School of Tokio College, Japan. Teacher of Police and law schools in Paotingfu. In 1910 Pien helped in organizing the Bureau of Judicial Affairs in Chekiang. Appointed Chief of the Bureau of Administrative Affairs in Chekiang. Was elected Vice-Speaker of the Provincial Assembly of Chihli in July, 1912, and later became Speaker up-to-date. Mr. Pien is also an Adviser to the Police Administration of Chihli, and Director-General of the Ling-Ching Mining Corporation, Tientsin. Was spoken of as one of the ringleaders of the Tientsin faction of the Paoting (Tsao Kun) party which was instrumental in bringing about the coup d'etat in Peking Sept., 1923. Manager, Ho Pei Jih Pao, Tientsin. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**PIEN SHOU-SUN** (Z. S. Bien), (卞壽孫).—Kiangsu. Born in Hupeh 1884. Studied in the Chentan College, Shanghai and Brown University, (U.S.A.) Ph.B. (in Economics) Member, Organization Commission, Bank of China and Secretary, 1912. Assistant, Department of Note Issue, 1913. Associate Chief Auditor, 1914. Chief Auditor, Aug. 1914. Organized the Chung Foo Union Bank, 1916, Auditor and Manager of its Tientsin Office, 1914. Assistant Manager Bank of China, Tientsin Office, 1918, Manager, 1919 to 1922. Transferred to Peking, 1922. Now Manager at Tientsin. Author of Education as a Means of Social Progress. Fifth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**PIEN YIN-CH'ANG** (alias Pien Yueh T'ing), (卞蔭昌字月庭).—Chihli. Born 1870. During the Manchu regime, Mr. Pien was a Senior Secretary of the Board of Works and of Board of Justice. Was elected Associate Director, Tientsin General Chamber of Commerce. When the Tientsin Merchants' Volunteer Corps was organized, he became its Commanding Officer. Member of Tientsin Red Cross Society. In 1913, Mr. Pien was elected Chairman of the Executive Committee of the United Chamber of Commerce of Chihli. In the same year, he was appointed President of Panama Exhibition Participation Committee. Counsellor to the former President Feng Kuo-chang, who was then Tutuh of Chihli, September, 1913. Adviser to the Civil Governor of Chihli. 1914. Became the President of the Association for the Protection of China's Sovereignty and Territory during the Lao Hsi Kai dispute. Was commissioned by the Minister of Finance to investigate taxation conditions in Chihli in connection with tariff revision, 1917. Was elected member of Parliament, August, 1918. President, Tientsin General Chamber of Commerce, November, 1919 up to date. President National Associated Chamber of Commerce. 2nd Class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**PIEN YUEH-TING** see Pien Yin-chang. — p. 1033 ([scan leaf 1084](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1084/mode/1up))

**P'ENG CHAO-HUANG, (彭兆璜)** .—Honan. Born May, 1873. For sometime studied in the normal school in Honan. Graduated from a Law College in Tokio, Japan. Chairman, Honan Provincial Assembly. Formerly of Kuomintang. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**P'ENG K'E-HO, (彭兆蒿)** .—Kweichow. Born Oct. 1893. Studied in the Wu Cheng Middle School (Peking) and graduated from a school in Japan. Vice-President (acting for the President), Kweichow Provincial Educational Association. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**P'ENG KUANG-LIEH, (彭光烈)** .—Szechwan. General of 2nd and 3rd Szechwan Army Divisions. Defence Commissioner of Chaokuang in Szechwan, July 1914. Military Councillor at the Chiangchünfu. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**P'ENG T'EO-SHENG** .—General. In command of troops on the Tibetan frontier, 1910-18. Besieged by Tibetans in Chamdo for three months; capitulated April, 1918. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**P'ENG YUNG-I, (彭允彝)** .—Appointed Acting Minister of Education Dec. 1922. Appointment confirmed Jan. 1923. First class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**PING K'E-CHUAN, ([illegible])** .—Fengtien. Born 1885. Studied police administration in Fengtien during Ching dynasty. Elected member of Fengtien Provincial Assembly, 1912. Became Member of Parliament Dec. 1912. Appointed Political Advisor to the Governor of Fengtien, 1914. Reelected member of Parliament 1918. Appointed Taoyin in Kirin, 1920. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**PO CH'ANG-CHIEH, (白常潔)** .—Shensi. Born 1870. Graduate of a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chinputang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**PO WEN-WEI, (柏文蔚)** .—Anhwei. Educated in the Military Academy, Wuchang. Commander of Battalion of 9th Division at Chinkiang, 1911. After the fall of Nanking took an expeditionary force to Pukow and defeated the Imperialists. Tutuh of Anhwei, July, 1912. Given rank of Chung Chiang (Lieutenant-General) in October, 1912. Frontier Commissioner of Shensi and Kansu, June 30, 1913. Deprived of all ranks for participation in the rebellion, July 27, 1913. High Military Adviser to the President. Member of Chiangchunfu. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**P'U LUN (Prince), (溥倫)** .—Manchu. Great-grandson of the Emperor Tao Kwang, who died in 1850. Prince of the Fourth Order. Vice-President of Imperial Clan Court, September, 1900. Lieutenant-General, Bordered Yellow Banner, February, 1901. Imperial Commissioner, St. Louis Exposition, 1904. Superintendent, Peking Octroi, September, 1907. President of the National Assembly during period of organization and its first session (September, 1907, to January 11, 1911). Appointed Minister of Agriculture, Commerce, and Industry, spring, 1911. Removed from office during the Revolution. Among Chinese he is the most popular member of the Imperial Clan. He was one of the few Princes who did not flee from Peking on the inauguration of the Republic. Represents Imperial Family at most Republican functions. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**P'U YI (溥儀) (HSUAN TUNG)** , Ex-Emperor.—Son of Prince Ch'un (Tsai Li) and nephew of the Emperor Kwang Hsu. Born on February 11, 1906. Succeeded to the Throne, under the Regency of his father, on November 14, 1908, and adopted the reign-title of Hsuan Tung. Abdicated on February 12, 1912. His mother was the daughter of the late Jung Lu. Under the Republic the ex-Emperor continued his studies under his old tutors and also received instruction from Mr. R.F. Johnston, C.B.E. On June 30, 1917, Chang Hsun carried out a monarchical coup in the interests of the Manchu Dynasty. Hsuan Tung ascended the throne, but the "monarchy" survived little more than a week and once again his name was affixed to an "abdication." Cut off his queue in May 1922. Married in December 1922. — p. 1034 ([scan leaf 1085](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1085/mode/1up))

**Sa Chen-ping, (薩鎮冰)** —Fukien. Born March 30, 1859. Brigade-General. Educated in Naval School, Foochow, and Royal Naval College, Greenwich, England, K.C.M.G. Admiral Commanding Peiyang Squadron, 1903. Admiral, Kwangtung, August, 1905. Commander-in-Chief, land and sea, Kwangtung, November, 1906. Land command withdrawn, January, 1908. Commissioner for Naval Reorganization, June, 1909. Vacated office, July, 1909. Admiral of fleet, July, 1909. Accompanied Prince Tsai Hsun on naval mission to Europe, Oct. 1909; and to Japan and America, August, 1910. In command of Imperial naval forces during Revolution. Took a naval force up to Hankow but owing to lack of supplies and continual defections among his crews was forced to vacate his command and escaped to North China. Director General of Land and River Police, Shanghai and Woosung, August 14, 1913. High Inspector of Kwangtung and Fukien, 1917. Appointed Minister of Navy, December 4, 1919. Sent on special mission to pacify Fukien, and appointed Civil Governor for Fukien, Oct. 15, 1922. Commissioned to enforce Anti-opium laws in Fukien and Anhwei, Sept. 1922. First class Chiaho and Wenhu decoration. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Sao-ke Alfred Sze** see Shih Chao Chi. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shang Chen-ku'ei, (尚鎮奎)** —Shensi. Born 1874. Graduate of a Technical School in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Teacher at a Middle School in Tungchow, and Chairman of the Educational Society of Taleihsien, and was the Representative of Tutuh Chang in Shanghai and Hankow during the Revolution. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shao Chang, (邵章)** —Chekiang. Hanlin. Judge, Administrative Court and is now (Feb. 1924) in charge of the Court. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shao Ch'ang-jung, (邵長鎔)** —Kiangsu. Born 1863. Member of Tzu-yiehu. Kiangsu, Industrial Secretary in the Tutuh's Yamen. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chinputang. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shen Chi-ch'ang, (沈其昌)** —Chekiang. Born 1881. Educated in Japan. President of the High Court of Justice in Kwangtung, 1915, of Shantung, Feb. 1917. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shen Chia-yi, (沈家彝)** —Kiangsu. Born 1882. President of the High Court of Justice in Fengtien, 1915. Presiding Judge, Supreme Court (Civil Division). — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shen Chin-chien, (沈金鑑)** —Chekiang. Civil Governor of Chekiang. Was summoned to Peking by President Li Yuan-hung, Oct., 1922. First Class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. Third Order of Merit. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shen Chun-ju, (沈鈞儒)** —Chekiang. Secretary of Ministry of Finance in the Ching Dynasty. Commissioner of Education, Chekiang, August, 1912. Member of Ministry of Civil Affairs. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shen Hung-yin, (沈鴻英)** —Kwangtung. Served in the Army in Kwangsi. Defence Commissioner in Kwangtung. Was appointed Tuli of Kwangsi, Mar. 20, 1923 under the influence of General Wu Pei-fu. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shen Jui-lin, (沈瑞麟)** —Chekiang. Attached to Waiwupu, 1908. Minister at Vienna, August, 1910. Appointed Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs, Jan. 5, 1922. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**Shen Pao-ch'ang, (沈寶昌)** —Chekiang. Born 1886. Chujen. Procurator, Peking during 1912. Secretary of Ministry of Justice. Magistrate of Shanghai, since 1915. — p. 1035 ([scan leaf 1086](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1086/mode/1up))

**SHEN YUN-P'EI, (沈雲沛)** —Kiangsu. Senior Secretary, Board of Commerce, January, 1907. Junior Vice-President do., March, 1908. Assistant Director, Tientsin-Pukow Railway, July, 1909. Acting President, Board of Communications, August 10. Acting Junior Vice-President, Board of Civil Appointments, January, 1911. Vacated office June, 1911, but retained post of Assistant Manager, Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Advisory Minister, Privy Council, August, 1911. Manager of Pukow-Hsinyang Railway, January 13, 1913. Was actively interested in Yuan Shih-kai's monarchical movement. — p. 1036 ([scan leaf 1087](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1087/mode/1up))

**SHIH CHAO-CH'ANG** —Chekiang. Born 1872. Chujen. Secretary to the Chinese Legation at St. Petersburg, 28th year of Kwang Hsu. Chinese Secretary to the Second Peace Conference at The Hague. First Secretary in the Chinese Legation at The Hague, and also First Secretary of the Chinese Legation in Rome. In the 1st year of Hsuan Tung he acted as Minister to Germany, and returned to China in the 2nd year. Is now Departmental Chief of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Author of Comment on the Sino-Russian Treaty. Chinese Consul-General in the Philippines, 1915. — p. 1036 ([scan leaf 1087](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1087/mode/1up))

**SHIH CHAO-CHI, (SAO-KE ALFRED SZE), (施肇基)** —Chekiang. Educated in America, at Cornell University. Acting Junior Secretary, Board of Communications, December, 1906. Removed from office, January, 1907. For some time Managing Director of the Luhan Railway. A Director of the Northern Railways, 1908. Customs Taotai, Harbin, spring, 1908. Acting Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Kirin, July, 1910. Junior Councillor, Board of Foreign Affairs August, 1910; Senior Councillor, August, 1911. Imperial Commissioner to the International Plague Conference at Mukden, April, 1911. Nominated Chinese Minister to America, Peru, Mexico, and Cuba just after the outbreak of the Revolution, but did not proceed. Minister of Posts and Communications, and for a time Acting Minister of Finance in the first Republican Cabinet. Resigned on account of ill-health in June. Is related, by marriage, to Tang Shao-yi. Appointed Officer of Ceremonies at the President's Office, December 27, 1913. Minister to Great Britain, June 20, 1914. Minister to Washington, 1921. Chinese delegate to the Washington Conference, Nov. 1921. Visited China, Nov. 1922. Nomination as Minister of Foreign Affairs rejected by Senate, Jan. 1923. Second Order of Merit. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 1036 ([scan leaf 1087](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1087/mode/1up))

**SHIH CHAO-TSENG, (施肇曾)** —Chekiang. Born 1868. Attaché to Chinese Legation, Washington, 1893-95. Consul, New York, 1896-97. Commissioner of the Shanghai-Nanking Railway, 1905-7. Assistant Director of the Peking-Hankow Railway, 1910-11. Director-General of the Lunghai Railways and Associate Director-General of the Tatung-Chengtu Railway, 1913-22. Second class Paokuang Chiaho and Second class Wenhu Decorations. — p. 1036 ([scan leaf 1087](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1087/mode/1up))

**SHIH KUO-CHEN, (施國楨)** —Kansu. Born September 25, 1886. Studied in the Provincial College (Kansu) and the Government Law School (Peking). Principal, Public Law School of Kansu. 1918 (?). Vice-Chairman, Provincial Educational Association of Kansu since June 1920. Formerly of Kuomintang. — p. 1036 ([scan leaf 1087](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1087/mode/1up))

**SHIH PIN, DR., (施斌)** —Second Secretary, Chinese Legation, Washington. Summoned by Minister Yen of Foreign Affairs to serve in the Ministry, Aug. 1921. — p. 1036 ([scan leaf 1087](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1087/mode/1up))

**SHIH LU-PEN, (施履本)** —Hupeh. Graduated from a Japanese University. Member of Ministry of Foreign Affairs since the Republic, became Special Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Shantung August, 1919 to Feb. 1923. Was on various occasions connected with the President's office. Acting Minister to Japan, 1923. Author of a Japanese word book. Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho, Tashou Chiaho and First class Tashou decorations. — p. 1036 ([scan leaf 1087](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1087/mode/1up))

**SHIH TSE-HSIEN, (史澤咸)** —Shantung. Born 1884. Studied in a University in Japan. During Revolution was appointed Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs in the Tutuh's office, and then a member of the Advisory Council in Nanking. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan, and a member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Has formed a Society for the Cultivation and Colonization of the Frontiers, and has raised a capital of more than $500,000 to start colonization and cultivation in Kirin and other places. Non-partisan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SHIH YU, (施愚)** —Szechwan. Chinshih. Educated in Europe. Councillor, Privy Council, August, 1911. Deputy Commissioner, Constitutional Department of Cabinet, February, 1912. Chief of Legislation Bureau and Chief of Bureau for Codification of Law, July, 1912. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SHU, S. S** see Hsü Shih-hsiang. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SHUI CHIH-HENG, (薛之珩)** —Police Superintendent of Peking since 1921. Second Class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SIA CHWANG-TING** see Hsieh Chuan T'ing. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SING TSEN-SENG** —Son of the Rev. Sing Eng-teh. Educated at the C.M.S. School and Trinity College, Ningpo, where he subsequently became a Master and Headmaster. Ordained Deacon, October 20, 1889. Pastor of Ningpo City, 1911. Archdeacon of the Diocese of Chekiang. Pastor at Hangchow, 1917. Consecrated as first Chinese Anglican Bishop (of the Chung Hua Sheng Kung Hui), October 2, 1918. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SOONG TSUNG-FAUNG** see Sung Chun-fang. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SU K'AN-SHIH, (粟戡時)** —Hunan. Born 1879. Studied in Japan. Was Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs, and Supervisor of Changsha and Yochow Customs. Resigned June, 1913, and since then he has been High Adviser to the Tutuh of Hunan. Was Vice-Speaker of Hunan Tzu Yi Chu. Non-partisan. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SU SHI-TI, (蘇錫第)** —Anhwei. Born 1876. Chujen. Appointed Vice-Minister of Finance, Feb. 1923. Resigned March, 1923. Again appointed to the same post, Nov. 1923. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SU WEI, (粟戚)** —Honan. Born February 6, 1876. Studied in Law in Japan. Superintendent of Changsha Customs and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Hunan since June 21, 1920. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SU YU-FANG, (蘇毓芳)** —Fengtien. A Graduate of the Law School in Fengtien. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. In the 2nd year of Hsuan Tung he organized the Tung Tze Hui, with the object of demanding a Parliament: he gathered together more than 5,000 students, and by their earnestness, Viceroy Hsi Liang promised to memorialize the Throne for them. Was imprisoned 27 days for being suspected as revolutionist, when securing arms for Pao An Hui. Chinputang. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SUN, C. Y** see Sun Chung Ying. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SUN CHEN-CHIA, (孫振家)** —Was a Taoyin in Hupeh. Recommended by Wang Chan-yuan to be Civil Governor of Hupeh, but was rejected by the people. Governor of the Metropolitan District. Co-Director of the Shantung Relief Bureau, May 19, 1922. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SUN CHI-CH'ANG, (孫其昌)** —Fengtien. Born 1884. Graduated in 1911 from a Japanese normal school. Was for some time Commissioner of Education in Heilungkiang. Appointed Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for the same province, July 14, 1921. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1037 ([scan leaf 1088](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1088/mode/1up))

**SUN CHING, (孫徽)** —Kiangsu. Born 1867. Chujen. Was once magistrate in Szechwan. Vice-Chairman, Kiangsu Provincial Assembly. Author of Peimen Poems and Notes on Japan (in Chinese). Formerly of Kung-hotang. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN CHING-CH'ING, (孫鏡清)** —Szechwan. Born 1884. Studied in Japan, and a Graduate of the Law College in Peking. Was Magistrate of Hsiangcheng, Honan, was one of the promoters of the Shanghai Chung Kuo Kung Hsueh; and was teacher to several Law Schools. Is now a lawyer and a member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Author of The Principles of the Constitution of the Ching Dynasty, etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN CH'UAN-FANG, (孫傳芳)** —Shantung. Studied Military Science in Japan, specializing in infantry tactics. Upon return to China, Sun was assigned to the 2nd Division of Peiyang Army. Adviser to Tuchun of Hupeh. Commander 21st brigade. Appointed Tuli of Fukien on Mar. 20, 1923, through Wu Pei-fu's insistence. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN CHUNG-YING, (C. Y. Sun), (孫仲英)** —Kiangsu. Until recently, was interested in business. Now Sun resides in Tientsin and is identified with many forms of social service. Notably in the Anti-opium propaganda and famine relief work of 1920-21. Sun also promoted and successfully conducted the United War Work Compaign of 1918. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN FENG-TSAO, (孫鳳藻 alias Sung Tse-wen 孫子文)** —Tientsin. President Chihli Fishery School, Tientsin. Became Director General of Tientsin-Pukow Railway Summer, 1922. Second class Wenhu decoration. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN HUNG-YI, (孫洪伊)** —Tientsin. Born 1870. Was elected member of the House of Representatives and has been Minister of Interior. Second order of Merit. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN PAO-CH'I, (孫寶琦)** —Hangchow, Chekiang. Born 1867. Yinsun. Member, Board of Justice and Taotai in Chihli. Minister to France, June, 1902. Chief Secretary, Government Council, summer, 1906. Governor of Peking, autumn, 1906. Minister to Germany, April, 1907 to December, 1908. Assistant Director, Tientsin-Pukow Railway, Sept. 1908. Returned to Peking, January, 1909. Acting Governor (June, 1909) and Governor (November, 1910) of Shantung. Shantung prematurely proclaimed its independence and Sun accepted the post of President of the Province. November 15, 1911. On November 29, two days after recapture of Hanyang, it returned to the Imperial fold, whereupon Sun tendered his resignation in a penitent memorial. Was pardoned by the Throne, but resigned in December, 1911. Director-General, Revenue Council, May 11, 1913. Minister of Foreign Affairs, concurrently acting premier September 11, 1913. Director Audit Office, 1915-1916. Minister of Finance, 1916. Director, Shui Wu Chu, 1916-23. Director, Government Famine Relief Bureau, June 17, 1922. Appointed Premier Jan. 12, 1924, the appointment being subsequently ratified by the parliament. First class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. Third Order of Merit. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN P'EI, (孫培)** —Anhwei. Born 1880. Studied in the Imperial University in Japan. A Councillor of Political Affairs to the President: a member of Committee in the Cabinet: a delegate for the Preparation of the National Assembly: and a Councillor of the Ministry of the Interior. Vice-Minister, 1922. A member of the Committee of the Chinputang. Author of the Spirit of the Constitution, etc. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**SUN SHIH-WEI, (孫世偉)** —Chekiang. Born 1882. Graduated from a Japanese University. B.S. Secretary to Governor of Chekiang, 1912. Commissioner of Industries for Chekiang, Feb. 1913; of Interior, Sept. 1913. Appointed Taoyin of Ting-Chang Tao, Fukien, 1914; Administrative Commissioner for Shansi, Sept. 1914. Was Acting Civil Governor of Shansi, Oct. to Nov. 1916, when became a Taoyin in that province. Administrative Commissioner for Honan, June 1917 to Sept. 1922. Is a Commissioner of the China International Famine Relief Commission. Wrote a book on Japanese government finance. Member of Chinputang. Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho and Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1038 ([scan leaf 1089](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1089/mode/1up))

**Sun Tan-lin, (孫丹林)** —Minister of Interior, May-Dec. 1922. President Commission for study of Yangtzekiang Conservancy, Sept. 1922. Appointed Tupan of Administration for Chengchow, March, 1924. First Class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. Second Order of Merit. — p. 1039 ([scan leaf 1090](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1090/mode/1up))

**Sun Tao-jen, (孫道仁)** —Hunan. Formerly expectant Taotai of Fukien. Transferred to the military service, and appointed Brigade-General of the Foochow-Kienningfu military circuit, and Commander of the Ninth Division. Elected Tutuh of Fukien on the outbreak of the Revolution. July 12, 1912. Resigned December 5, 1913. Commissioned to enforce Anti-opium laws in Kansu and Sinkiang, Sept. 1922. — p. 1039 ([scan leaf 1090](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1090/mode/1up))

**Sun Tao-yeh C** see Sun To-yu. — p. 1039 ([scan leaf 1090](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1090/mode/1up))

**Sun To-sen, (孫多森)** —Anhwei. Born 1868. Intendant of Industry, Chihli, March, 1910; vacated, January, 1912. Industrial Commissioner, Anhwei, May, 1912; vacated May, 1912. Manager, Bank of China, December 23, 1912. Chief Civil Administrator and additionally Acting Tutuh, Anhwei, June 30, 1913. Relieved of both posts, July 27, 1913. Manager, Bank of China, June, 30—July 21, 1917. — p. 1039 ([scan leaf 1090](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1090/mode/1up))

**Sun To-yu,, (Taoyuh C. Sun), (孫多鈺)** —Anhwei. Born 1876. Educated in America. Engineer and Director General, Kirin Changchun Railway. Director General, Nanking-Hunan Railway; Shanghai-Nanking Railway; Chuchow-Chingchow Railway, etc. Vice-Minister of Communications, Jan. 30, 1923. — p. 1039 ([scan leaf 1090](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1090/mode/1up))

**Sun Tse-wen** see Sun Feng-tsao. — p. 1039 ([scan leaf 1090](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1090/mode/1up))

**Sun Wen, (Sun Yat-sen), (孫文)** —Kwangtung. Born in 1866, the son of a farmer in Hsiangshan district. Learnt English at an early age, and studied under Dr. Kerr of the American Mission. Enrolled as a student of the Alice Memorial Hospital at Hongkong in 1887, whence he graduated as "Licentiate of Medicine and Surgery, Hongkong," in 1892. Started to practise in Macao, where he organised the Young China Party. Subsequently settled in Canton, where he became an active revolutionary. After the failure of a conspiracy at Canton in 1895 he fled to Macao, and thence proceeded to Hongkong, Japan, Honolulu and America, in all of which places he obtained adherents to the Reform Party. Arrived in England in 1896, and on October 11, of that year was kidnapped outside the Chinese Legation by orders of the Chinese Minister. It was intended to ship Dr. Sun to China as a lunatic, but he managed to make his plight known to Dr. Cantlie, who was instrumental in effecting his release after twelve days' imprisonment. Subsequently Dr. Sun toured through Europe, America and the East as a revolutionary propagandist. In Japan (with General Huang Hsing) he was instrumental in founding the T'ung Meng Hui. Was in England when the Wuchang outbreak occurred, but came out to China at the end of 1911, and was elected Provisional President of the Republic by the Nanking Council. Resigned from the Presidency on the abdication of the Manchus, on the understanding that Yuan Shih-kai should be elected to succeed him, and proceeded on a tour to Wuchang and South China, where he advocated a socialistic policy. Came to Peking at the President's request in August, 1912, and was accorded an enthusiastic welcome. Advocated an extensive programme of railway construction, and on September 10. was appointed by the President "to consider and draft plans for a national system of railways," and to "submit and discuss the same with international financiers." Visited Kalgan, and on September 17, left for Taiyuanfu and Shanghai. Strongly advocated the transfer of the capital from Peking to Wuchang or Nanking. His authority as Chief of the National Railway Corporation was cancelled on the outbreak of the rebellion, and Dr. Sun subsequently took up his residence in Japan. Dr. Sun was in Shanghai in 1920, but proceeded to Canton in 1921 when the Kwangsi officials were ejected by General Chen Chiung-ming, and was "elected" "President" of China by the so-called Parliament there in April 1921. He was expelled from Canton by Chen Chiung-min in the summer of 1922, and returned to Shanghai, where he remained until Feb. 1923, when he again established himself in Canton. Author of Elementary Treaties on People's right, Sun Wen Hsueh Shuo and Industrial Plans. — p. 1039 ([scan leaf 1090](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1090/mode/1up))

**SUN WU, (孫武)** —Hupeh. A noted Hupeh revolutionary, whose accident with a bomb in the Russian Concession at Hankow in October, 1911, led to the discovery of the revolutionary headquarters, and precipitated the Revolution. Sun Wu was injured by the explosion, and owed his life to the devotion of his friends, who succeeded in concealing him in a place of safety until he had recovered. He subsequently played a prominent part in the events of the Revolution at Wuchang, becoming Chief of the Military Department in that city. It was mainly through his instrumentality that the counter-revolutionary plot hatched at Wuchang in August was frustrated. Educated at the Military Academy, Wuchang. After a visit to Japan he returned and opened schools with the object of spreading revolutionary ideas. Connected with the Changsha riots of 1910. In February, 1911, when in Wuchang, he arranged with Huang Hsing for a simultaneous outbreak in Wuchang and Canton in May. The latter plot miscarried, but Sun Wu's opportunity came with the subsequent railway unrest. Instrumental in forming the Kunghotang Party. First Class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1040 ([scan leaf 1091](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1091/mode/1up))

**SUN YAT-SEN** see Sun Wen. — p. 1040 ([scan leaf 1091](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1091/mode/1up))

**SUN YUN-YU, (孫潤宇)** —Kiangsu. Born 1878. A Graduate of Peiyang University and a Law School in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan and the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Kuomintang. Author of Outlines of Administration, etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1040 ([scan leaf 1091](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1091/mode/1up))

**SUNG CH'UN-FANG, (Soong Tsung-faung), (宋春舫)** —Chekiang. Studied in Genoa, Switzerland. Professor, Peking National University. Secretary, Ministry of Finance. Deputy Chief of Loan Department, Ministry of Finance. Author of "Parcourtant le Monde en Flammes" (1917), "La Litterature Chinoise Contemporaine" (1919) and some dramatic works. — p. 1040 ([scan leaf 1091](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1091/mode/1up))

**SUNG FA-HSIANG, (Fartsan T. Sung), (宋發祥)** —Fukien. Born August 26, 1883. Educated in America. B.Sc., M.A., (Ohio Wesleyan) B.Sc., (Chicago). Teacher, Fukien Provincial College. Professor of Chemistry, Government University, Peking, 1908-12. Technical Expert Ministry of Finance, 1914-1916. Co-director, Assaying Office, 1912-13. Director, Soochow Mint and Co-Director Bureau of Engineering. 1914. Co-Director, Bureau of Printing and Engraving, 1916. Inspector General of Mints, 1914-16. Adviser to Tuchun of Kiangsu, 1915-16. Director, Nanking Mint, 1917. Political Councillor to the President since 1919. Author of Qualitative Analysis of Important Metals. Third class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1040 ([scan leaf 1091](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1091/mode/1up))

**SUNG, FARTSAN T** see Sung Fa Hsiang. — p. 1040 ([scan leaf 1091](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1091/mode/1up))

**SUNG HSIAO-LIEN, (宋小濂)** .—Kirin. Acting Commissioner of Internal Affairs, Heilungkiang, March, 1911, and Commissioner, December, 1911. Tutuh of Heilungkiang, 1914. Member of Tsanchengyuan, May 1914. Director, Chinese Eastern Railway, 1920. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**SZE, ALFRED** see Shih Chao Chi. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**SZE HSI-CHANG, (姒錫章)** .—Chekiang. Born 1870. Chinshih. Graduated from a Japanese Law School, and has been magistrate of Hsunhua and of Tientsin before becoming Taoyin of Tsin-hai-tao, Chihli in 1919. Second class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**SZE-TU YING, (司徒穎)** .—Kwangtung. Born 1878. Studied in the Imperial Peking University. Chujen. Member of Provisional Senate, 1912. Member of Chung Yi Yuan. Commissioner of Industries of Kansu since 1917. Author of books in Chinese on Mining and Industry. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**TAI CH'EN-LIN, (戴陳霖)** .—Chekiang. Studied in language schools in Shanghai and Peking. Graduated from a College in France. Attached to Chinese Legation at Paris. Councillor of the Waichiaopu, April, 1912. Chinese Minister at Madrid. Appointed Minister to Sweden, Norway and Denmark, June 16, 1922. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**TAI CHIH-CH'IEN, (Tse-chien Tai) (戴志騫)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1888 Graduate, St. John's University, Shanghai, 1912. A.B. University of the State of New York, Albany Library School, 1918. B.L.S. Teacher to a middle school in Wenchow, 1907. Librarian, St. John's University, 1904-14. Assistant Librarian, Camp Upton Library, Camp Upton. Long Island, N.Y., 1918-19. Librarian, Tsing Hua College, Peking, 1914 to date. Acting Business Manager, Tsing Hua College, 1922. Author of Bibliography of 250 books on China with descriptive and critical notes; and many magazine articles. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**TAI, TSE-CHIEN** see Tai Chih-chien. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**TAN CHEN, (覃振)** .—Hunan. Graduate of a Law College in Japan, a member of the Tung Meng Hui. Secretary to the Tutuh of Hupeh. Member of Chung Yi Yuan. Was imprisoned for five years for promoting the Revolution in Hunan. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**T'AN JUI-LIN, (譚瑞霖)** .—Kwangtung. Born 1885. Graduate of Preliminary Normal School Kwangtung. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. After having graduated he travelled in the Straits Settlements, where he established two Primary Schools. During the Revolution he drove away the Imperialist troops from Hsinhuihsien in response to the call of the Lieutenant-General Hwang Min-tang. Was elected Speaker of the Town Council of the Hsien. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**T'AN SHOU-K'UN** .—Hupeh. Born 1876. After the Revolution was appointed Secretary to the Tutuh of Kwangtung. Resigned the secretaryship, and became Sub-Editor of the Min Lik Pao, Shanghai. Then became Editor of the Chung Pao at Wuchang. Was Military Adviser to the Tutuh of Hupeh, and Speaker of the Hupeh Assembly. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**T'AN YEN-K'AI, (譚延闓)** .—Hunan. Obtained degree of Chinshih, and in 1904, at the early age of twenty-five, was appointed a Hanlin Compiler. Retired from official life shortly afterwards, and lived in his native district until the formation of the Hunan Provincial Assembly, of which he was elected Chairman. Was elected Chief of the Military Department of Hunan on the outbreak of the Revolution, but after the assassination of two rival Tutuhs he was elected to that office in his native province. Relieved of his post, October 13, 1913; became Tuchun of Hunan, Aug. 1916. Again Tuchun and Civil Governor, Dec. 1918. Was succeeded by Chao Heng-ti as Commander-in-Chief of Hunan Allied troops. Appointed Minister of Interior, June 12, 1922, but did not accept office. Second order of Merit. — p. 1041 ([scan leaf 1092](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1092/mode/1up))

**T'ANG CHI-YAO, (唐繼堯)** Yunnan. Acting Tutuh of Kweichow, April, 1912. Commander-in-Chief of Yunnan and Kweichow troops for the relief of Szechwan, August 26, 1913. Acting Tutuh of Yunnan, September 28, 1913. Acting Chief Civil Administrator of Yunnan, October 4, 1913. Chiangchun of Yunnan, 1915; subsequently Tuchun and Civil Governor until 1921, when he was driven out of the Province. Was Chief of the General Staff in the Canton Government, 1918. Returned to Yunnan again in March 1922. — p. 1042 ([scan leaf 1093](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1093/mode/1up))

**T'ANG CHUN** Deputy Governor, Bank of China, August 21, 1913; Governor, do., September 25, 1913. — p. 1042 ([scan leaf 1093](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1093/mode/1up))

**T'ANG CHUNG, (湯中)** Kiangsu. Councillor, Ministry of Education, August, 1912. Acting Vice-Minister of Education, 1923. — p. 1042 ([scan leaf 1093](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1093/mode/1up))

**T'ANG ER-HO, (湯爾和)** Received medical training abroad. Was Acting Minister of Education in Wang Ching-hui's Cabinet, 1922. First Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 1042 ([scan leaf 1093](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1093/mode/1up))

**T'ANG HSIANG-MING, (湯薌銘)** Hupeh. Educated at the Liang Hu College, Wuchang, and obtained degree of Chujen before leaving for France to undergo a course of naval training. After graduating from a Naval College he served on a French warship, and returned to China in the autumn of 1911. A nephew of Tang Hua-lung. Appointed Vice-Minister of the Navy on the formation of the first Republican Cabinet. Vice-Admiral, Nov. 3, 1912. Acting Tutuh of Hunan, October 24, 1913. Chiangchun and Civil Governor of Hunan, 1914. Appointed Civil Governor of Hupeh, July 7, 1922, but was not allowed to assume office by the Tuchun and formally relieved of post Jan. 8, 1924. Appointed Tupan for Port development for Wuchang, Hanyang and Shakow, Dec. 1923. First class Tashou Chiaho Decoration. — p. 1042 ([scan leaf 1093](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1093/mode/1up))

**T'ANG SHAO-YI, (唐紹儀)** Kwangtung. Educated in America. Secretary to Yuan Shih-kai while the latter was Imperial Resident in Korea. Consul-General in Korea after the Chino-Japanese war. Then employed on the staff of the Northern Railway Administration. In Shantung with Yuan Shih-kai, winter, 1900. Customs Taotai, Tientsin February, 1902. Special Commissioner to Tibet, September, 1904. Proceeded to India as special Envoy, to negotiate the Tibet Convention, which was subsequently completed at Peking in April, 1906. Acting Junior Vice-President of the Board of Foreign Affairs, November, 1905. Substantive Junior Vice-President of the Board of Foreign Affairs, February, 1906. Director-General Shanghai-Nanking, and Lu-Han Railways, 1906. Comptroller-General of Revenue Council, May 1906. Senior Vice-President of Board of Communications, November, 1906. Continued to act as Vice-President of Board of Foreign Affairs. First Governor of Fengtien on reorganization of government of Manchuria, April, 1907. Special Envoy to America to thank the Government for waiving part of the Boxer Indemnity, July, 1908. Resigned Governorship of Fengtien, July, 1909. Expectant Vice-President Board of Communications, and Acting President, August, 1910, and resigned in the spring. Appointed Minister of Communications on the dismissal of Sheng Hsuan-huai on October 26, 1911. Proceeded to Shanghai as Yuan Shih-kai's delegate to negotiate with the revolutionary leaders in December. Resigned his position as delegate on December 27. Appointed Premier, after abdication of the Manchus on February 12. Resignation as Premier accepted on June 27, when he was appointed Superior Adviser to the President on State Affairs. A member of the Tung Meng Hui. One of the four Directors of Canton Government, 1918. Minister of Finance at Canton, 1919-22. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. Li Yuan-hung appointed Tang Premier, Aug. 5, 1922 to succeed Dr. Yen. Tang refusing to come up to Peking, his appointment was cancelled on Sept. 19th whereupon Dr C. H. Wang was made Premier. — p. 1042 ([scan leaf 1093](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1093/mode/1up))

**T'ANG TIEH-CHIAO, (湯鐵樵)** —Hunan. Born 1877. Studied in Hunan and graduated from a law college in Japan. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Justice, October 1913. Appointed a Councillor, Ministry of Justice, 1913. Third class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 1043 ([scan leaf 1094](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1094/mode/1up))

**T'ANG TSAI-CHANG, (唐在章)** —Kiangsu. Born 1887. Educated—Japan. Upon return to China, was Secretary in the President's Office, 1911, and later Secretary to the Cabinet Office. Became actively interested in the Tramway System of Peking. A member of Commission for Re-Adjustment of National Finance, 1923. — p. 1043 ([scan leaf 1094](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1094/mode/1up))

**T'ANG TSAI-FU, (唐在復)** —Kiangsu. Born 1877. Graduate of Kwang Fang Yen College, Shanghai; of the Tungwen College, Peking; and the Paris University. Secretary to the Chinese Legation at Paris and The Hague, and at one time Acting Chinese Minister in France, Holland, and Russia. Was Vice-President of the Board of Foreign Affairs in the last Dynasty. Junior Secretary Waichiaopu, March, 1912; Councillor, Waichiaopu, April, 1912. Minister to Italy, 1922. — p. 1043 ([scan leaf 1094](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1924-5/page/n1094/mode/1up))
